# 32. Script Bank --- Weather and Road Conditions

Weather is not an automatic crisis beat. It is racing context first. The
hosts should enjoy the way conditions change competition unless the
simulation produces actual danger/consequence.

## Rain arrives before a race

**MARA:** There it is.

**JULES:** Rain.

**MARA:** And suddenly every note we made about this race twenty minutes
ago needs an asterisk.

**JULES:** Not every note.

**MARA:** Most of mine.

**JULES:** That's because most of yours were predictions.

**MARA:** Correct. `{EVENT_NAME}` in the wet. Who does this help?

**JULES:** Depends what everybody brought, what they're running, how
quickly the surface changes, and whether the drivers believe the grip is
where it was five minutes ago.

**MARA:** So your answer is "watch the race."

**JULES:** Usually a strong strategy.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Rain catches a race already underway

**TOMMY:** Rain is coming down here now.

**MARA:** Mid-race?

**TOMMY:** Mid-race.

**JULES:** That's fun.

**MARA:** For us.

**JULES:** Correct.

**TOMMY:** `{SUPPORTED_LIVE_CONDITION_FACT}`.

**MARA:** All right. Everybody just got a new race without stopping the
old one.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Cold road / tires outside useful window

**NIA:** Watch `{MACHINE_OR_DRIVER}` over the opening section.

**MARA:** Why?

**NIA:** `{SUPPORTED_TIRE_OR_ROAD_TEMP_FACT}`.

**JULES:** So don't judge the first corner like it's the fifth.

**NIA:** Exactly. Temperature is part of the machine whether people like
thinking about it or not.

**MARA:** And if they push too early?

**NIA:** Then physics provides feedback.

**MARA:** Engineering has such threatening vocabulary.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Overheated tires

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` looked good early and now
`{SUPPORTED_HANDLING_OR_PACE_FACT}`.

**NIA:** We have `{SUPPORTED_TIRE_TEMP_EVIDENCE}`.

**JULES:** Which does not mean we know every setup decision behind it.

**NIA:** Correct. But we can say the tire state isn't where they want
it.

**MARA:** So the fastest way through the first half can become the
slowest way through the second.

**NIA:** Welcome to tires.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Fog

**MARA:** I cannot see very far down that route.

**TOMMY:** Neither can I.

**JULES:** Visibility is `{SUPPORTED_VISIBILITY_OR_WEATHER_FACT}`.

**MARA:** This is one of those races where being fast and being certain
are gonna fight each other.

**JULES:** They often do.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Sandstorm / pollution / strange atmospheric condition

**MARA:** Night City has decided the road itself wasn't enough.

**JULES:** `{WEATHER_STATE}`.

**MARA:** `{EVENT_NAME}` is still scheduled under it.

**JULES:** And conditions are conditions. Everybody gets the same sky.

**MARA:** Not necessarily the same answer to it.

**JULES:** Exactly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 33. Script Bank --- Fuel

## Somebody runs short

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` has a fuel problem.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That is such a miserable way to lose speed.

**JULES:** Why?

**MARA:** Because the car still works. The driver still works. The race
is still right there. You're just running out of the thing that lets any
of it matter.

**JULES:** Fuel strategy is still strategy.

**MARA:** I know. I'm allowed to hate good strategy when it hurts
somebody interesting.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Refueling becomes the race

**MARA:** This is gonna come down to fuel, isn't it?

**JULES:** It might.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_CONTEXT}`.

**JULES:** Carry more and you carry the weight. Carry less and you need
the stop or the margin.

**MARA:** So every liter is both useful and annoying.

**JULES:** That's a surprisingly good summary.

**MARA:** Thank you. I'm learning.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Fuel-saving drive works

**MARA:** They made it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_RESULT}`.

**MARA:** I thought they were gonna have to stop.

**JULES:** So did I.

**MARA:** Write that down.

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** Jules was wrong, entry number `{RUNNING_JOKE_IF_RETAINED}`.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Stranded for fuel

**MARA:** That's it for `{DRIVER}`?

**JULES:** Looks like fuel if the recorded reason is
`{SUPPORTED_FUEL_DNF}`.

**MARA:** Brutal.

**JULES:** The lightest fuel load in the world is very fast right until
it isn't.

**MARA:** Put that on the shirt.

**JULES:** We closed the shirt department.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

**JULES:** You enter a machine, that machine is part of the entry.

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` never made the start.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REASON_IF_KNOWN}`.

**MARA:** That's the kind of result that barely feels like a result.

**JULES:** Still goes in the record.

**MARA:** Which is cruelly efficient.

## Grid delay

**MARA:** We're still waiting.

**TOMMY:** Still waiting.

**MARA:** How long have you been standing there?

**TOMMY:** Long enough to have opinions about everybody's idle sound.

**JULES:** Best one?

**TOMMY:** I'm not starting that war.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

**JULES:** Reset grid is `{SUPPORTED_RESTART_STATE}`.

**MARA:** I always wonder what that does to a driver. You already spent

**JULES:** And now you have to spend it correctly a second time.

**MARA:** That sounds medically dubious.

## Fixed opening after a rollover

The next continuity's/new Week's fixed opening remains cheerful and
unchanged if the fiction treats it as a fresh Race Week without literal
host memory.

That contrast is intentional.

The player may carry the emotional memory of the previous Week.

Mara and Jules do not secretly acknowledge it.

**The fixed opening becomes more powerful because the show does not know
why it hurts.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 51. Runtime Editorial Rule --- Hold the Thread

This section is **canonical implementation guidance** for the eventual
deterministic commentary scheduler.

The show must have a train of thought.

A beat being factually eligible does **not** mean it is editorially
eligible to interrupt what the show is currently doing.

If Mara and Jules begin covering a particular race, story, interview,
technical discussion, incident, or argument, the scheduler should
normally let that thread reach a natural handoff point before selecting
unrelated material.

## Core invariant

**Eligibility answers: "Could they say this?"**

**Editorial continuity answers: "Would they say this now?"**

Both must be true.

A deterministic radio that chooses the highest-scoring eligible line
every few seconds will sound deranged even if every individual line is
perfectly factual.

## Race ownership

When the show commits to a race, that race becomes the active editorial
thread.

A typical lifecycle can be:

`PREVIEW → GRID → START → LIVE DEVELOPMENT → FINISH → RESULT/REACTION → HANDOFF`

Not every race needs every stage. The show may join late or leave after
a concise result. But once it has made a meaningful commitment,
unrelated ordinary race commentary should not steal the microphone
halfway through.

Example of **bad scheduling**:

> MARA: Gonzalez is defending the lead through the final section---
>
> JULES: Meanwhile, Chen qualified sixth in Northside.
>
> MARA: And a new power unit is on the Exchange.
>
> TOMMY: Gonzalez wins!

All facts may be valid. The show is still broken.

Example of **correct scheduling**:

> MARA: Gonzalez is defending the lead through the final section---
>
> TOMMY: `{SUPPORTED_FINAL_LIVE_FACT}`.
>
> JULES: This is going to the line.
>
> TOMMY: Gonzalez wins!
>
> MARA: What a finish.
>
> \[Result reaction / short cooldown / handoff.\]
>
> MARA: All right. While that was happening, we also got a qualifying
> result from Northside.

The other story waits and becomes a clean transition.

## Story ownership beyond races

The same rule applies to:

-   severe incidents
-   interviews
-   Garage discussions
-   standings arguments
-   Moody/policy discussions
-   economic stories
-   team changes
-   Deep/flight discoveries
-   rescue operations
-   championship clinches
-   memorial/aftermath coverage
-   long host conversations

A three-minute Garage discussion should not be punctured by an ordinary
P8 finish elsewhere simply because that finish generated a valid
trigger.

## Interrupt classes

Some events **can** break an active thread.

Possible hard-interrupt classes:

-   verified emergency/public-safety instruction
-   genuinely severe live incident
-   race currently being covered reaches a decisive live moment
-   major official cancellation/suspension affecting the active subject
-   urgent rescue status where delay would make the broadcast
    nonsensical

Possible soft-interrupt classes, usually queue instead:

-   another race result
-   qualifying result
-   market sale
-   sponsor announcement
-   routine DNF
-   new entry
-   standings update
-   ordinary R&D release
-   routine Moody interpretation
-   nonurgent news

## Queue and decay

Queued stories should not all be dumped afterward.

Each candidate needs editorial lifetime.

A result can become:

-   immediate live story
-   delayed recap
-   one-line scoreboard mention
-   material for "One Thing You Missed"
-   part of later standings discussion
-   stale enough to drop entirely

**The show is allowed to miss things.**

That is a feature. Race Week is larger than the broadcast.

## Segment closure

Authored beats should expose natural closure/handoff points where
possible.

Examples:

-   `THREAD_OPEN`
-   `THREAD_CONTINUE`
-   `THREAD_CAN_HANDOFF`
-   `THREAD_CLOSED`

Exact implementation is coder territory. The requirement is behavioral,
not a mandate for these literal field names.

## Relevance is not permission to derail

A new fact about the same driver does not automatically belong in the
current conversation.

A new fact about the same machine does not automatically belong.

A new fact about the same location does not automatically belong.

The current beat has a subject and purpose. New material should enter
only if it naturally advances that subject.

## Simultaneous races

The broadcast may consciously choose one race over another.

If another important race finishes while the show is committed:

> **MARA:** While we were with Gonzalez in Japantown, `{WINNER}` won
> `{OTHER_EVENT}`. We'll get you that result in a second.

This preserves the illusion of a finite broadcast team watching an
enormous event.

## Long-form commitment

Some authored conversations may run several minutes.

That is intentional.

Do not chop them apart merely to increase event responsiveness.

If an urgent event interrupts, the show may later return:

> **MARA:** Before we got pulled away, Nia was making a point about
> `{TOPIC}`.

That return itself is authored continuity.

## Scheduler principle

**Do not schedule lines. Schedule attention.**

Once attention is placed somewhere, preserve it until the story
finishes, becomes stale, is deliberately handed off, or is legitimately
interrupted.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 67. Script Bank --- Interviews as Deterministic Authored Packages

If interviews are added, they must be authored conversations keyed to
facts, not generated freeform personality simulation.

## Winner interview shell

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`, you just won `{EVENT_NAME}`.
`{SUPPORTED_RESULT_CONTEXT}`. What was the race from where you were
sitting?

The driver's response must come from an authored eligible beat whose
factual predicates match the race.

Possible authored response families:

-   controlled favorite
-   surprised first-time winner
-   exhausted endurance winner
-   angry winner after contact
-   relieved winner after prior DNF
-   technical driver focused on machine
-   terse driver
-   exuberant driver

Do not synthesize an answer from hidden personality stats at runtime.

## Interview refusal

A driver may have an authored refusal/short-answer state if supported by
their authored characterization.

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`, quick word?

**DRIVER:** Not right now.

**TOMMY:** Fair enough. Back to you.

No invented explanation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

These are authored errors with authored correction, never accidental

# 76. Pre-Variant-Pass Canonical Reminder

The breadth corpus now contains many examples, seeds, and working
scenes.

Before implementation:

-   These are authored **conversation objects**, not a bag of
    interchangeable lines.
-   The coder must preserve thread continuity.
-   The scheduler chooses **attention**, then appropriate authored
    material within that attention.
-   Do not fire every eligible beat.
-   Do not bounce between races merely because another fact scores
    highly.
-   Do not treat every `{SUPPORTED_*}` slot as mandatory.
-   Do not expose internal provenance labels in player-facing speech.
-   Do not infer facts that the audit forbids.
-   Do not make Day 1 secretly know Day 3.
-   Do not make ordinary good racing congratulate itself for avoiding
    disaster.
-   Do not make the player the center of races they are not part of.
-   Do not make the show omnipresent. Missing a story is allowed.
-   Do not compress long conversations merely because shorter event
    barks are easier to schedule.
-   Urgent interruption must be exceptional and authored.
-   After an interruption, returning to the prior thread is allowed and
    often desirable.
-   A race the show commits to should normally be seen through its
    natural finish or deliberate handoff.
-   **Coherence across time is more important than maximizing trigger
    utilization.**

This document is intended to become a canonical repo document. Treat its
behavioral invariants as requirements and its working dialogue as
authored source material subject to later variance/editing passes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 77. Variance Pass --- Canonical Strategy

This begins the first dedicated variance pass.

The purpose is **not** combinatorial sentence generation.

The purpose is to give recurring simulation situations multiple authored
ways to become radio, while preserving the temporal arc, current Race
Week condition, recent broadcast history, and the hosts' train of
thought.

Race Week's temporal loop is an advantage. The show does not need
infinite dialogue. It needs a bounded corpus large enough that:

-   a normal player playing one or two hours at a time rarely feels the
    machinery underneath;
-   a long-session player can leave the show running because the next
    treatment remains interesting;
-   a new Race Week can reuse scenario families with different
    simulation facts and still feel meaningfully different;
-   repeated facts do not always summon the same conversation;
-   the same fact can mean something different on Day 1, Day 2, Day 3,
    or late Week;
-   the same fact can mean something different in a healthy Week and a
    collapsing Week;
-   callbacks make reuse feel like continuity rather than repetition.

## Variance axes

A beat family can vary along several independent axes:

**Temporal position** - D1 opening - D1 established - D1 night - D2 -
D3 - D4+

**Race Week condition** - Expected / healthy - Slightly below
expectations - Subpar - Bad - Catastrophic

**Recent history** - no related precedent - same thing happened
recently - opposite thing happened recently - hosts predicted it - hosts
predicted against it - intervention occurred - recovery occurred -
previous attempt failed

**Sporting context** - ordinary race - series/championship relevance -
favorite - underdog - rookie/new name - established recurring name -
player - NPC - machine with history - location with history

**Broadcast context** - studio already discussing this race -
trackside - recap - result missed live - queued after another thread -
quiet-period long form - short handoff before next event

**Host posture** - Mara leads - Jules leads - Tommy leads - Imani
factual spine - Nia technical spine - Celeste economic spine - Darnell
local spine - genuine disagreement - shared excitement -
restrained/quiet

These axes choose among **whole authored variants**. They do not
authorize assembling dialogue from arbitrary fragments.

## Repetition policy

A beat does not become forbidden merely because it has played before.

Human radio repeats structures, jokes, questions, and habits.

What should be controlled is conspicuous repetition.

Useful metadata conceptually includes:

-   times played this Week
-   last played
-   last variant used
-   recent semantic family
-   recent host lead
-   recent punchline/joke family
-   whether a callback has matured
-   whether the underlying simulation facts materially differ

A strong variant may repeat in a later Race Week. That is desirable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 78. Variance Family --- Ordinary Race Finish

This will be one of the most frequently used families in the entire
corpus.

A normal finish in a healthy Week should usually sound like sports
radio, not a narrative event.

## FINISH-NORMAL-A --- Winner first

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` across first! `{P2}` second, `{P3}` third.

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` gets `{EVENT_NAME}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_DECISIVE_RACE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Good race. Put that one on the board.

Use when the result itself is the main story.

## FINISH-NORMAL-B --- Performance first

**MARA:** That's a really good drive from `{WINNER}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PERFORMANCE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** They earned that one. `{P2}` second, `{P3}` third.

Use when a supported performance detail is more interesting than
ceremony.

## FINISH-NORMAL-C --- New name

**MARA:** New one for me: `{WINNER}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Well, now I know the name. `{WINNER}` wins `{EVENT_NAME}`.

Best early in Week or when the driver has little prior show history.

## FINISH-NORMAL-D --- Familiar name

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` again.

**JULES:** Not necessarily another win if the prior familiarity came
from another result. But yes, we know the name.

**MARA:** And now we know it with a win attached.

Use only with actual prior show history.

## FINISH-NORMAL-E --- Tommy owns it

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` gets there! `{SUPPORTED_FINISH_FACT}`.

\[Trackside breath.\]

**TOMMY:** That's `{EVENT_NAME}`.

**MARA:** Nice call, Tommy.

**TOMMY:** Nice race.

Useful when the show has been live with the race and does not need
studio restatement.

## FINISH-NORMAL-F --- Result after show joined late

**MARA:** We only caught the end of this one, but `{WINNER}` takes it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RESULT_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** We'll get the full classification when it's final.

Useful when attention entered late.

## FINISH-NORMAL-G --- Result missed live

**MARA:** While we were with `{PRIOR_THREAD}`, `{WINNER}` won
`{EVENT_NAME}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_NOTABLE_RESULT_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That's one we owe a little more time later.

May queue a recap. Do not pretend the show witnessed it.

## FINISH-NORMAL-H --- Quick scoreboard treatment

**MARA:** `{EVENT_NAME}` is final: `{WINNER}`, `{P2}`, `{P3}`.

**JULES:** Next up, `{NEXT_THREAD}`.

Use when the race is low salience and the show has somewhere else to go.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 79. Variance Family --- Close Finish

## CLOSE-A --- Pure excitement

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` by `{MARGIN}`!

**MARA:** That's nothing!

**JULES:** It's `{MARGIN}`.

**MARA:** You know what I mean.

## CLOSE-B --- Sympathy for second

**MARA:** `{MARGIN}`. That's gonna hurt.

**JULES:** `{P2}` did almost everything required to win.

**MARA:** Almost is such an ugly word in racing.

## CLOSE-C --- Winner perspective

**MARA:** Imagine seeing the line and knowing `{P2}` is right there.

**JULES:** `{WINNER}` found enough.

**MARA:** Exactly enough.

## CLOSE-D --- Late Week callback

**MARA:** Not these two again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_HEAD_TO_HEAD}`.

**MARA:** And now `{WINNER}` gets them by `{MARGIN}`.

Only when actual history supports "again."

## CLOSE-E --- Bad Week, sport briefly cuts through

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` by `{MARGIN}`!

\[Beat.\]

**MARA:** That was a hell of a finish.

**JULES:** Yeah.

**MARA:** I'm glad we got to call that.

The surrounding Week is bad, but the line does not force a crisis
discussion into the result.

# 80. Variance Family --- Dominant Win

## DOMINANT-A --- Admiration

**MARA:** Nobody had an answer for `{WINNER}`.

**JULES:** Margin is `{MARGIN}`.

**MARA:** That's a statement.

## DOMINANT-B --- Technical curiosity

**JULES:** I want to look at this one afterward.

**MARA:** Because?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PERFORMANCE_FACT}`. That margin did not appear
from nowhere.

**MARA:** Careful.

**JULES:** I said I want to look. I did not say I know why.

## DOMINANT-C --- Championship threat

**MARA:** If you're racing `{WINNER}` in `{SERIES}`, that result gets
your attention.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POINTS_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** Hard to ignore somebody disappearing up the road.

## DOMINANT-D --- Early Week restraint

**MARA:** Big win.

**JULES:** One event.

**MARA:** I know. I'm still circling the name.

## DOMINANT-E --- Familiar dominance

**MARA:** We've seen this movie.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REPEAT_DOMINANCE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Somebody needs a different ending.

Only with genuine repeated dominance.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 81. Variance Family --- DNF / Mechanical Retirement

## DNF-A --- Matter-of-fact

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` is out.

**JULES:** Official reason: `{SUPPORTED_DNF_REASON}`.

**MARA:** That's their race.

## DNF-B --- Good position lost

**MARA:** Oh, that's painful. `{DRIVER}` was `{POSITION}`.

**JULES:** And now `{SUPPORTED_DNF_REASON}`.

**MARA:** Nothing you can do with points you don't finish.

## DNF-C --- Repeat trouble

**MARA:** Again?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REPEAT_DNF_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** That's becoming the story of their Week.

Use only when the repeat is substantial enough to support the statement.

## DNF-D --- After prior recovery

**MARA:** They worked all the way back from `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_PROBLEM}`
for this.

**JULES:** Racing does not care how much work the previous result cost.

**MARA:** Cold.

**JULES:** True.

## DNF-E --- Low salience

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` recorded DNF, `{SUPPORTED_DNF_REASON}`.

Then move on. Not every retirement earns grief.

## DNF-F --- Bad Week

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` is out. `{SUPPORTED_DNF_REASON}`.

**JULES:** Driver status?

**IMANI/TOMMY:** `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

If safe/ordinary, return to the race. A bad Week makes them check, not
automatically catastrophize.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 82. Variance Family --- Upset

## UPSET-A --- Mara celebrates uncertainty

**MARA:** Yes!

**JULES:** You picked `{WINNER}`?

**MARA:** No. That's why I'm happy.

**JULES:** Explain.

**MARA:** The Week surprised me.

## UPSET-B --- Jules protects sample size

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` just beat `{FAVORITE}`.

**JULES:** In this event.

**MARA:** I can hear the asterisk.

**JULES:** Good.

## UPSET-C --- Odds context

**CELESTE:** `{WINNER}` was `{SUPPORTED_ODDS}`.

**MARA:** That's an upset with a receipt.

**CELESTE:** Correct.

## UPSET-D --- Favorite had actual problem

**MARA:** Upset, but with context.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_FAVORITE_PROBLEM}`.

**MARA:** Still counts.

**JULES:** Absolutely. Just don't erase the race that produced it.

## UPSET-E --- Late Week championship effect

**MARA:** That's not just an upset anymore.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** That's a points-table earthquake.

Use sparingly; not every change is an earthquake.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 83. Variance Family --- Penalty

## PENALTY-A --- Mara hates it

**MARA:** I hate results changing after the line.

**JULES:** You hate penalties.

**MARA:** I hate *this* penalty.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PENALTY_FACT}`.

## PENALTY-B --- Jules defends enforcement

**JULES:** If the rule exists, this is when it matters.

**MARA:** I know.

**JULES:** Then?

**MARA:** Understanding isn't liking.

## PENALTY-C --- Classification chaos

**MARA:** Give me the order again.

**IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_FINAL_OR_PROVISIONAL_CLASSIFICATION}`.

**MARA:** Thank you. The road gave me one podium and Race Control gave
me another.

## PENALTY-D --- Driver still wins

**MARA:** Penalty and still the win.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_MARGIN_AFTER_PENALTY}`.

**MARA:** That's one way to make the argument irrelevant.

## PENALTY-E --- Penalty costs title/major standing

**MARA:** That's enormous.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** One rule, one decision, and the whole board moves.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 84. Variance Family --- Quickhack

## HACK-A --- Routine permitted attack

**MARA:** `{ATTACKER}` goes after `{TARGET}` with `{HACK}`.

**JULES:** Legal in this event.

**MARA:** And now `{TARGET}` has two races: the road and the network.

## HACK-B --- Defense

**MARA:** Denied.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_DEFENSE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** I love a door slamming shut.

## HACK-C --- Attack changes position

**MARA:** That hack bought `{SUPPORTED_POSITION_EFFECT}`.

**JULES:** Direct sporting consequence.

**MARA:** Nobody gets to call that decorative.

## HACK-D --- Repeated attacker

**MARA:** `{ATTACKER}` again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PATTERN}`.

**MARA:** At this point, if you're sharing a grid with them, you know
the question is coming.

Do not claim every opponent literally knows unless public/observable
context supports it. This can be Mara's inference if phrased
accordingly.

## HACK-E --- Restricted/prohibited event

**IMANI:** Race Control is reviewing `{SUPPORTED_HACK_EVENT}` under
`{SUPPORTED_RULE}`.

**MARA:** So now the hack is racing and potentially the penalty is
racing.

**JULES:** Correct.

## HACK-F --- Bad Week sensitivity

**MARA:** I know it's permitted. I know that.

**JULES:** But?

**MARA:** After `{SUPPORTED_RECENT_CONTEXT}`, I am having a harder time
enjoying watching somebody attack a moving car.

This is Mara's evolving opinion, not a rule claim.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 85. Variance Family --- Player Appears

Player-specific commentary should be memorable but sparse enough that
the show does not become a personalized announcer strapped to the
player's bumper.

## PLAYER-ENTRY-A --- Early unknown

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` on this grid.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PLAYER_HISTORY_OR_NONE}`.

**MARA:** Let's see what they are.

## PLAYER-ENTRY-B --- Known podium finisher

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` again. We know they can get on a podium.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_PODIUM}`.

**MARA:** New event. New question.

## PLAYER-ENTRY-C --- Known winner

**MARA:** There's `{PLAYER_NAME}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_WIN_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** All right. Now I pay attention when that name shows up.

## PLAYER-ENTRY-D --- After repeated DNFs

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is back.

**JULES:** After `{SUPPORTED_DNF_PATTERN}`.

**MARA:** Finish first. Then worry about being brilliant.

This is host advice/opinion, not direct address.

## PLAYER-ENTRY-E --- After long absence

**MARA:** Haven't said that name in a while.

**JULES:** First start since `{SUPPORTED_LAST_START}`.

**MARA:** Welcome back to the board.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 86. Variance Family --- Player Result

## PLAYER-WIN-A --- First win

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` wins!

**JULES:** First one.

**MARA:** There it is. Now the Week has their name in the winners
column.

## PLAYER-WIN-B --- Repeat win

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_WIN_COUNT_OR_PATTERN}`.

**MARA:** Okay. That's no longer an introduction.

## PLAYER-WIN-C --- Unexpected machine

**MARA:** In *that*?

**JULES:** `{MACHINE}`.

**MARA:** I mean that affectionately. `{PLAYER_NAME}` just won `{EVENT}`
in `{MACHINE}`.

## PLAYER-PODIUM-A

**MARA:** Podium for `{PLAYER_NAME}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Good points. Good day.

## PLAYER-DNF-A

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is out.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REASON}`.

**MARA:** That's Race Week. Next one.

## PLAYER-DNF-B --- Painful late DNF

**MARA:** No. Not there.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_LATE_DNF_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** They had the result.

**JULES:** They had most of the race.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 87. Variance Family --- Standings Segment

## STANDINGS-A --- D1 curiosity

**MARA:** I know it's early. I don't care. Show me the board.

**JULES:** `{LEADER}`, `{P2}`, `{P3}`.

**MARA:** Tiny sample. Large opinions.

## STANDINGS-B --- D2 memory

**MARA:** Yesterday's results have survived the night.

**JULES:** That's generally how standings work.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_BOARD_CONTEXT}`.

## STANDINGS-C --- D3 pressure

**MARA:** This is where a bad finish stops being an anecdote.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POINTS_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** The board is collecting debts now.

## STANDINGS-D --- Late Week arithmetic

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}` needs `{SUPPORTED_REQUIREMENT}`.

**MARA:** There. That's the whole race in one sentence.

**JULES:** Not the whole race.

**MARA:** You know what I mean.

## STANDINGS-E --- Wide open

**MARA:** Nobody owns this.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CLOSE_STANDINGS}`.

**MARA:** Good. Keep it messy.

## STANDINGS-F --- Dominant leader

**MARA:** Somebody has to start taking points out of `{LEADER}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_LEAD}`.

**MARA:** Otherwise we're gonna run out of Week before they run out of
lead.

# 88. Variance Family --- Weather

## WEATHER-RAIN-A --- Excited

**MARA:** Rain!

**JULES:** You sound delighted.

**MARA:** I'm indoors.

## WEATHER-RAIN-B --- Technical

**NIA:** Road state just changed the problem.

**MARA:** Tires first?

**NIA:** Tires, braking, visibility, temperature, driver confidence.
Pick one.

## WEATHER-RAIN-C --- Already troubled Week

**MARA:** Of course it's raining.

**JULES:** Weather has not joined a conspiracy.

**MARA:** Let me be dramatic for five seconds.

## WEATHER-FOG-A

**MARA:** Tommy, what can you see?

**TOMMY:** Less than I could.

**MARA:** Excellent field report.

## WEATHER-SAND-A

**JULES:** `{WEATHER_STATE}` at `{LOCATION}`.

**MARA:** Every race needs at least one thing the setup sheet didn't
personally ask for.

# 89. Variance Family --- Fuel

## FUEL-A --- Strategy admiration

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}` made the number work.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_FACT}`.

**JULES:** That's not glamorous. It is racing.

## FUEL-B --- Running dry

**MARA:** No, no, no.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_FAILURE}`.

**MARA:** Worst sound in racing is an engine that has nothing left to
burn.

## FUEL-C --- Light-load gamble

**MARA:** They traded fuel for speed.

**JULES:** If the evidence supports the load strategy, yes.

**MARA:** And now we find out whether the bill arrives.

## FUEL-D --- Late Week resource pressure

**MARA:** Fuel wasn't the story on Day One.

**JULES:** It is for `{DRIVER_OR_TEAM}` now.

**MARA:** That's the Week getting longer than the plan.

Only when actual history supports resource pressure.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 90. Variance Family --- Machine Returns

## MACHINE-RETURN-A --- Simple recognition

**MARA:** I know that car.

**JULES:** `{MACHINE}`.

**MARA:** Good. My brain still works.

## MACHINE-RETURN-B --- After repair

**MARA:** They fixed it.

**JULES:** They returned it to competition. We know that much.

**MARA:** Nia has trained you too well.

## MACHINE-RETURN-C --- After spectacular prior result

**MARA:** `{MACHINE}` is back after `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_RESULT}`.

**JULES:** Different event.

**MARA:** Same reason I'm watching.

## MACHINE-RETURN-D --- After failure

**MARA:** I want to see this finish.

**JULES:** Because of `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_FAILURE}`.

**MARA:** Yeah. Machines get redemption arcs too.

**JULES:** Editorially.

**MARA:** Let me have the car story.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 91. Variance Family --- R&D / New Part

## RND-A --- Curiosity

**NIA:** `{PUBLIC_RELEASE}` is now public.

**MARA:** Who tries it first?

**NIA:** That is the interesting question.

## RND-B --- Jules excited

**JULES:** Oh, that's clever.

**MARA:** You haven't seen it race yet.

**JULES:** I said clever, not fast.

## RND-C --- Mara skeptical

**MARA:** How much does it cost?

**CELESTE/NIA:** `{SUPPORTED_COST}`.

**MARA:** It had better do something entertaining.

## RND-D --- Immediate evidence

**NIA:** First useful result attached to `{PUBLIC_RELEASE}`:
`{SUPPORTED_RESULT}`.

**JULES:** Now we have something to discuss.

## RND-E --- Hype correction

**MARA:** Everybody wants to call `{PART}` the answer.

**NIA:** We have `{SUPPORTED_EVIDENCE}`. We do not have "the answer."

**MARA:** Fine. The interesting question.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 92. Variance Family --- Infrastructure Response

## INFRA-A --- Optimistic

**MARA:** They changed it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** Good. Learn fast.

## INFRA-B --- Skeptical

**MARA:** Is that enough?

**JULES:** We don't know yet.

**MARA:** Then this race is the test.

## INFRA-C --- It works

**MARA:** Same problem, better outcome.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** That's Race Week learning.

## INFRA-D --- It creates another problem

**MARA:** Fixed one thing.

**JULES:** Changed another.

**MARA:** City engineering as whack-a-mole.

## INFRA-E --- Late Week normalization

**MARA:** I forgot this corner didn't look like this on Day One.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ACCUMULATED_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** That's unsettling.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 93. Variance Family --- Team / Sponsor / Money

## TEAM-A --- New team, Mara

**MARA:** New colors on the board: `{TEAM}`.

**CELESTE:** `{SUPPORTED_TEAM_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Welcome to the expensive part.

## TEAM-B --- New team, Celeste

**CELESTE:** Somebody turned ambition into payroll.

**MARA:** Team?

**CELESTE:** `{TEAM}`.

## SPONSOR-A --- Straight

**CELESTE:** `{SPONSOR}` signs with `{TEAM_OR_DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** Result of the Week?

**CELESTE:** We know the deal exists. We do not know every motive behind
it.

## MONEY-A --- Purse matters

**CELESTE:** `{SUPPORTED_PAYOUT}`.

**MARA:** That's enough to change somebody's Week.

**CELESTE:** Potentially. Don't spend it for them.

## MARKET-A --- Machine sells

**MARA:** Somebody bought `{MACHINE}`.

**CELESTE:** `{SUPPORTED_TRANSACTION}`.

**MARA:** I hope they know what they're inheriting.

**JULES:** A machine.

**MARA:** And history.

# 94. Variance Family --- Quiet Period

## QUIET-A --- Relief

**MARA:** Nothing starts for `{WINDOW}`.

**JULES:** You sound emotional.

**MARA:** I can finish a sentence.

## QUIET-B --- Technical rabbit hole

**MARA:** We have time.

**JULES:** Dangerous phrase.

**MARA:** Nia, explain `{TOPIC}` until somebody stops you.

**NIA:** Happily.

## QUIET-C --- Standings rabbit hole

**MARA:** Give me the board.

**JULES:** All of it?

**MARA:** We have time.

## QUIET-D --- Bad Week silence

**MARA:** We have a gap.

**JULES:** Good.

\[Pause.\]

**MARA:** I don't want to fill it yet.

**JULES:** Then don't.

## QUIET-E --- Late Week exhaustion

**MARA:** Twenty minutes.

**JULES:** Sleep.

**MARA:** That's not how radio works.

**JULES:** Cowardice.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 95. Variance Family --- Day Opening

## D1-OPEN

The fixed Groundhog-Day opening remains intentionally recognizable.

Variance begins **after** the fixed anchor.

## D2-OPEN-A --- Healthy

**MARA:** Day Two. Yesterday gave us `{HEADLINE_A}`, `{HEADLINE_B}`, and
a standings board I am already too attached to.

## D2-OPEN-B --- Competitive

**JULES:** Day Two starts with `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** No warm-up?

**JULES:** We had a whole day.

## D2-OPEN-C --- Recovering

**MARA:** Day Two, and I want a better day than yesterday.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RESPONSE}` is in place.

**MARA:** Then let's see if it works.

## D2-OPEN-D --- Bad

**MARA:** Day Two. Race Week is continuing under `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

**JULES:** Today's calendar currently has `{SUPPORTED_CALENDAR_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That's where we start.

## D3-OPEN-A --- Healthy

**MARA:** Day Three. I officially know enough names to be dangerous.

**JULES:** You were dangerous before.

## D3-OPEN-B --- Championship

**MARA:** Day Three, and the board finally has teeth.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_CONTEXT}`.

## D3-OPEN-C --- Troubled

**MARA:** Day Three. No speech. Here's what we know.

**IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

## D3-OPEN-D --- Recovering

**MARA:** Yesterday ended better than it started.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RECOVERY_FACT}`.

**MARA:** I would like more of that.

# 96. Variance Family --- Day Closing

## D1-CLOSE-A --- Exhilarated

**MARA:** That was one day?

**JULES:** Yes.

**MARA:** Ridiculous. Do it again tomorrow.

## D1-CLOSE-B --- Reflective

**MARA:** This morning everybody was a prediction.

**JULES:** Now?

**MARA:** Results.

## D1-CLOSE-C --- Slightly bruised

**MARA:** Good day. Not a perfect day.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_TROUBLE}`.

**MARA:** Fix that tomorrow and I'll take it.

## D1-CLOSE-D --- Bad

**MARA:** Tomorrow has to be better.

**JULES:** Yeah.

## D2-CLOSE-A --- Healthy

**MARA:** Day Two was better than Day One.

**JULES:** Based on?

**MARA:** My enjoyment.

**JULES:** Finally, a clearly labeled metric.

## D2-CLOSE-B --- Escalating

**MARA:** I keep waiting for the Week to settle down.

**JULES:** It may not.

**MARA:** I know.

## D3-CLOSE-A --- Healthy

**MARA:** Three days and I don't want it to stop.

**JULES:** Good problem.

## D3-CLOSE-B --- Exhausted/troubled

**MARA:** Three days feels like a month.

**JULES:** Go sleep.

**MARA:** You first.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 97. Variance Family --- Condition Tint Without Rewriting the Race

The same underlying sporting beat can receive a small number of
**whole-scene condition variants**.

Do not inject condition adjectives into every line.

Example underlying fact:

`{WINNER}` wins `{EVENT}` by `{MARGIN}`.

## Healthy

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` by `{MARGIN}`. Great finish.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RACE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Put another one on the board.

## Slightly below expectations

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` by `{MARGIN}`. Good race.

**JULES:** Nice to get the conversation back onto the road.

**MARA:** Yeah. `{SUPPORTED_RECENT_TROUBLE}` can wait a minute.

Only if that trouble is already an active show concern.

## Subpar

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` gets it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RACE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That's a result worth giving its own space. We'll go back to
`{BROADER_WEEK_TOPIC}` after this.

## Bad

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` wins `{EVENT}`.

**JULES:** Good drive.

**MARA:** It was. We're still waiting on `{SUPPORTED_BROADER_STATUS}`.
For the moment, congratulations to `{WINNER}`.

## Catastrophic

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` is across.

\[Pause.\]

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` wins.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RESULT_FACT}`.

**MARA:** All right. Back to Imani.

Notice that the sporting facts do not change. **Attention and emotional
bandwidth change.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 98. Variance Family --- Severe Incident

This family needs substantial variance because repeating one solemn
script would become grotesque.

Every version remains fact-bound.

## SEVERE-A --- Shock

**MARA:** Wait.

\[Pause.\]

**MARA:** We have `{VERIFIED_EVENT}`.

**JULES:** Confirmed?

**IMANI:** Confirmed.

**MARA:** Okay. Stay with what we know.

Best for first major rupture.

## SEVERE-B --- Immediate factual mode

**IMANI:** We are interrupting race coverage for `{VERIFIED_EVENT}` at
`{LOCATION}`.

**MARA:** What is confirmed?

**IMANI:** `{VERIFIED_FACTS}`.

**MARA:** That's all we say.

Useful after the show has learned restraint.

## SEVERE-C --- Tommy witnessed

**TOMMY:** I saw `{SUPPORTED_OBSERVATION}`.

**MARA:** Tommy, only what you actually saw.

**TOMMY:** That's what I'm giving you.

**JULES:** Driver status?

**TOMMY/IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

## SEVERE-D --- Cause unknown

**MARA:** Everybody is going to want a cause immediately.

**JULES:** We don't have one.

**MARA:** Then we don't give them one.

## SEVERE-E --- Repeated bad day

**MARA:** Again.

\[Pause.\]

**JULES:** Yeah.

**MARA:** `{VERIFIED_EVENT}`.

No speech needed merely because another severe event happened.

## SEVERE-F --- Anger

**MARA:** I'm angry.

**JULES:** At what?

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_OBJECT_OF_ANGER}`.

The target of anger must be something factual, such as a published
decision or repeated known failure, not invented blame.

## SEVERE-G --- Jules leads

**JULES:** Stop. We need to correct the record before this goes any
further.

**MARA:** Go.

**JULES:** We know `{FACT_A}`. We do **not** know `{UNRESOLVED_CAUSE}`.

**MARA:** Thank you.

Useful when technical speculation would be tempting.

## SEVERE-H --- Silence

**IMANI:** `{VERIFIED_UPDATE}`.

\[Long pause.\]

**MARA:** All right.

Sometimes this is enough.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 99. Variance Family --- Recovery After Trouble

## RECOVERY-A --- Cautious

**MARA:** Better.

**JULES:** So far.

**MARA:** I'll take "so far."

## RECOVERY-B --- Infrastructure credit

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_RESPONSE}` went in, and since then
`{SUPPORTED_IMPROVEMENT}`.

**JULES:** Correlation caveat.

**MARA:** Fine. Still encouraging.

## RECOVERY-C --- Sport returns

**MARA:** You know what we haven't talked about for twenty minutes?

**JULES:** Safety policy.

**MARA:** Exactly. Wonderful.

## RECOVERY-D --- Mara allows herself to enjoy it

**MARA:** I was waiting for the other shoe all morning.

**JULES:** And?

**MARA:** I'm done waiting. That was a good race.

## RECOVERY-E --- Not fully healed

**MARA:** Better doesn't mean forgotten.

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** But better matters.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 100. Variance Family --- Broadcast Thread Handoffs

Variance is also needed in transitions so the show does not sound
mechanically segmented.

## HANDOFF-A --- Race to News

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` gets the result. Imani, you've got something for
us.

## HANDOFF-B --- Race to Garage

**MARA:** Keep `{MACHINE}` in your head. Nia, I want to talk about what
we just saw.

## HANDOFF-C --- Race to standings

**JULES:** That changes the board.

**MARA:** Then let's look at the board.

## HANDOFF-D --- Long conversation to race

**TOMMY:** Sorry to ruin the philosophy hour. They're lining up.

**MARA:** Perfect timing.

## HANDOFF-E --- News back to sport

**MARA:** That's the update. We'll stay on it. Right now, `{EVENT}` is
on the grid.

## HANDOFF-F --- Missed result catch-up

**MARA:** Before we move on, one result we missed while we were with
`{PRIOR_THREAD}`.

## HANDOFF-G --- Quiet reset

\[Stinger.\]

**MARA:** All right. New race.

Simple is useful.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 101. Variance Family --- Host Lead Rotation

The same subject should not always begin with Mara.

Example subject: unusual machine result.

## MARA LEAD

**MARA:** I need to talk about `{MACHINE}`.

**JULES:** I assumed you would.

## JULES LEAD

**JULES:** `{MACHINE}` just did something interesting.

**MARA:** That's your excited voice.

## NIA LEAD

**NIA:** Before either of you decide what `{MACHINE}` means, here's what
we actually observed.

## TOMMY LEAD

**TOMMY:** You two need to see this thing when it comes back around.

## CELESTE LEAD

**CELESTE:** You know what makes `{MACHINE}` more interesting? Its
price.

This alone multiplies texture without changing facts.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 102. Variance Family --- Recurring Jokes Must Evolve

Recurring jokes are valuable because repetition can become character
continuity.

But exact repetition should usually advance.

## "Radio"

First use:

**MARA:** I can hear your face.

**JULES:** We're on radio.

Later:

**MARA:** Don't say it.

**JULES:** Radio.

**MARA:** I said don't.

Later still:

**MARA:** Your face---

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** I didn't even---

**JULES:** We have done this.

Late Week:

**MARA:** I can hear your face.

\[Pause.\]

**JULES:** I'm too tired.

**MARA:** That's how I know Race Week is ending.

## Shirts

Early:

**MARA:** Put that on a shirt.

**JULES:** No.

Later:

**MARA:** Shirt.

**JULES:** Department closed.

Later:

**NIA:** Put that on a shirt.

\[Pause.\]

**MARA:** See?

**JULES:** I regret introducing you.

Callbacks should have state, not merely repeat a catchphrase forever.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 103. Variance Family --- Race Week Loop Reuse

A new Race Week may select previously heard variants.

That is not failure.

The loop itself provides changed context:

-   different driver
-   different machine
-   different location
-   different weather
-   different result
-   different prior history
-   different Week condition
-   different player involvement
-   different timing

Example:

Week A:

> **MARA:** New one for me: Belov. Well, now I know the name.

Week B:

> **MARA:** New one for me: Mayorga. Well, now I know the name.

The structure repeats. The simulation gives it new meaning.

However, high-salience long scenes should have broader variance than
low-salience utility scenes.

Suggested authoring priority:

1.  severe incidents / aftermath
2.  Day openings and closings
3.  player milestones
4.  championship/title moments
5.  first flight / Deep / reentry
6.  major quickhack events
7.  major policy/Moody changes
8.  ordinary finishes
9.  DNFs / penalties / qualifying
10. utility handoffs and scoreboard reads

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 105. Variance Pass II --- Qualifying

Qualifying is frequent enough that it cannot always be "surprise pole"
or a dry grid read. It can be its own small sporting story, or merely
setup for the race.

## QUAL-A --- Straight pole

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` has pole for `{EVENT}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_QUALIFYING_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Front of the line. Now keep it.

## QUAL-B --- Favorite confirms expectation

**MARA:** There you go. `{FAVORITE}` on pole.

**JULES:** Board liked them, clock liked them.

**MARA:** Race still gets a vote.

## QUAL-C --- Unknown/new name

**MARA:** Who is `{DRIVER}`?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PUBLIC_DRIVER_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Pole is a good introduction.

## QUAL-D --- Tiny margin

**MARA:** `{MARGIN}` between first and second.

**JULES:** That's enough.

**MARA:** Technically, yes. Emotionally, insulting.

## QUAL-E --- Favorite buried

**MARA:** `{FAVORITE}` starts `{POSITION}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_QUALIFYING_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Well. Now they've got something to do.

## QUAL-F --- Machine catches Jules's eye

**JULES:** `{MACHINE}` is `{POSITION}`.

**MARA:** You sound surprised.

**JULES:** I had a different expectation based on
`{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_EVIDENCE}`.

**MARA:** Clock says update the expectation.

## QUAL-G --- Player pole

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` on pole.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PLAYER_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** All right. Clean road if they can keep it.

## QUAL-H --- Player poor qualifying

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` has work to do from `{POSITION}`.

**JULES:** Race is longer than qualifying.

**MARA:** Usually why we run it.

## QUAL-I --- Late Week qualifying has history

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` on pole again at `{LOCATION_OR_SERIES}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RELEVANT_PRIOR_QUALIFYING_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** That's becoming familiar.

## QUAL-J --- Qualifying missed by show

**MARA:** We missed qualifying while we were with `{PRIOR_THREAD}`.
`{DRIVER}` has pole, `{P2}` alongside.

**JULES:** Race starts `{START_TIME}`.

**MARA:** There. Caught up.

# 106. Variance Pass II --- Starts and Restarts

## START-A --- Clean launch

**TOMMY:** They're away!

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_START_FACT}`.

**JULES:** Everybody through the first argument.

Use early in a race without implying danger was expected.

## START-B --- Pole sitter loses lead

**TOMMY:** `{P2_OR_OTHER}` gets `{POLE_DRIVER}` off the start!

**MARA:** Pole lasted exactly that long.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POSITION_CHANGE}`.

## START-C --- Mid-grid launch

**MARA:** Watch `{DRIVER}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POSITIONS_GAINED}` off the start.

**MARA:** That's how you erase qualifying in one breath.

## START-D --- Player gains places

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is moving already.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_START_GAIN}`.

**MARA:** Good morning.

## START-E --- Player loses places

**MARA:** Bad launch for `{PLAYER_NAME}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_START_LOSS}`.

**MARA:** Now earn it back.

## START-F --- Restart tension

**MARA:** Everybody gets to pretend the first start didn't happen.

**JULES:** The record can. Their nervous systems can't.

**TOMMY:** Reset is ready.

## START-G --- Second restart

**MARA:** Again?

**TOMMY:** Again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RESTART_REASON}`.

**MARA:** Fine. Race Week, take three.

## START-H --- Bad Week restraint

**TOMMY:** They're away.

**MARA:** All right. Let's have a race.

No ominous prediction. The weariness is enough.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## BATTLE-I --- Player defending

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` has company.

**JULES:** `{ATTACKER}` for `{POSITION}`.

**MARA:** Don't look in the mirror too long.

Host advice, not direct address.

## BATTLE-J --- Player attacking

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is there.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ATTACK_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** One good chance. Don't waste it.

# 110. Variance Pass II --- Provisional Results and Investigations

## PROV-B --- Long review

## PROV-C --- Result stands

**IMANI:** Review complete. Classification stands.

**MARA:** Now congratulations, `{WINNER}`.

## PROV-D --- Result changes

**IMANI:** Final classification changes `{SUPPORTED_RESULT_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** There it is.

**JULES:** Road result and official result part company.

Queue unless directly relevant to the active race.

When returned to:

`{EVENT}` review.

**IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_DECISION}`.

This is an explicit example of thread continuity overriding trigger
immediacy.

Disagreement variants should not reduce the hosts to permanent
ideological slots.

mind?

**JULES:** Especially then. Fix the rule afterward if you need to.

**MARA:** That is how you get monsters.

**JULES:** Sometimes monsters are informative.

## ARGUE-C --- Favorite

**JULES:** `{DRIVER_B}`.

**JULES:** I do.

**JULES:** It creates another competitive system.

**MARA:** It creates a competitive system inside a car doing
`{SUPPORTED_SPEED_OR_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** And now?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ACCUMULATED_EVIDENCE}` changed the question.

**MARA:** I'm not gonna gloat.

**JULES:** Thank you.

# 120. Variance Pass II --- Interviews

Interviews are authored packages with deterministic eligibility. They
can become a major variance reservoir later.

## INTERVIEW-A --- First-time winner, overwhelmed

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`, first recorded win. What are you feeling?

**DRIVER:** I don't know yet.

**TOMMY:** Fair.

**DRIVER:** Ask me when my hands stop shaking.

Only assign to an authored driver/response profile that permits it.

## INTERVIEW-B --- Technical winner

**TOMMY:** Where did you win that?

**DRIVER:** Braking. Car was good there all day.

**TOMMY:** Anything change before the start?

**DRIVER:** `{SUPPORTED_PUBLIC_SETUP_RESPONSE_OR_AUTHORED_NONANSWER}`.

## INTERVIEW-C --- Terse

**TOMMY:** Big win.

**DRIVER:** Yeah.

**TOMMY:** Anything for us?

**DRIVER:** Car was good.

**TOMMY:** All right. Mara?

## INTERVIEW-D --- Angry after contact

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`, you won, but `{SUPPORTED_CONTACT}`---

**DRIVER:** I won.

**TOMMY:** You did.

**DRIVER:** That's my answer.

Only where authored characterization supports this style.

## INTERVIEW-E --- Loser gracious

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`, `{MARGIN}` short.

**DRIVER:** They beat me.

**TOMMY:** That simple?

**DRIVER:** Today, yeah.

## INTERVIEW-F --- No interview

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}` is not stopping.

**MARA:** Then leave them alone.

No motive invented.

Then continue. Not every correction needs a joke.

## ERROR-B --- Wrong event sheet

**JULES:** Because your desk is a crime scene.

## ERROR-C --- Name stumble

**MARA:** `{DRIVER_NAME_ATTEMPT}`--- no, give me that again.

Then correct using authoritative pronunciation/name data if available.

## ERROR-D --- Jules technical correction

**JULES:** That's `{INCORRECT_AUTHORED_TERM}`. No, sorry,
`{CORRECT_TERM}`.

**NIA:** I was going to let you suffer.

## ERROR-E --- Serious correction

**MARA:** Correction from us: earlier we said
`{INCORRECT_AUTHORED_STATEMENT}`. The confirmed fact is
`{CORRECT_FACT}`.

**JULES:** That's the record.

Serious factual corrections should be clean, not cute.

## CALLBACK-A --- Prediction right

**MARA:** I said `{SAFE_PRIOR_PREDICTION}`.

**MARA:** For hours.

## CALLBACK-B --- Prediction wrong

**JULES:** You had `{DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** I was young and foolish.

**JULES:** Forty minutes ago.

## CALLBACK-C --- Jules wrong

**MARA:** Say it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_NEW_RESULT}` contradicted my expectation.

**MARA:** No, the other words.

**JULES:** I was wrong.

**MARA:** Beautiful medium, radio.

## CALLBACK-D --- Old joke returns under different stakes

A joke from D1 can return in D3 but should not be forced into a solemn
moment. Context gate required.

## CALLBACK-E --- Opinion evolves

**MARA:** I used to think `{PRIOR_AUTHORED_POSITION}`.

**JULES:** What changed?

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_HISTORY}`.

This is one of the strongest ways to make the 72-hour show feel alive.

Exact clock semantics remain dependent on canonical Race Week time
implementation.

**JULES:** Still awake?

**MARA:** Extremely.

**JULES:** Liar.

\[Stinger.\]

**MARA:** That's a day.

**JULES:** One down.

Do not interrupt the race to announce the day change.

After the race:

**MARA:** Somewhere in that battle, Day Two started.

**JULES:** Appropriate.

This is a direct application of thread ownership.

## DAYBOUND-D --- Bad Week

**MARA:** Day `{DAY}`.

\[Beat.\]

**MARA:** Here's where things stand.

Then facts. No ceremonial joke required.

**MARA:** Day `{DAY}`.

**JULES:** You checked?

**MARA:** Twice.
