# 143. Variance Pass II --- Week Rollover Contrast

The fresh Week opening does **not** vary based on the previous Week's
trauma unless cross-Week host memory is explicitly made canonical later.

The player's memory supplies the contrast.

A fixed cheerful opening after a terrible prior Week can be more
emotionally powerful than an authored wink.

Do not write:

-   "Here we go again."
-   "Let's hope this one goes better."
-   "Last Week was rough."
-   reset jokes
-   déjà vu jokes
-   causal-loop awareness

unless a separate persistent-history rule later explicitly authorizes
them.

The loop is **structural knowledge for the game**, not necessarily
character knowledge.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 144. Variance Pass II --- Anti-Repetition Selection Guidance

The runtime should avoid the uncanny pattern where different facts
repeatedly choose the same surface treatment.

Conceptual selection memory:

-   last N beat families heard
-   last N variants heard
-   last host lead
-   last emotional posture
-   last joke/callback family
-   last segment type
-   last active driver/machine/location focus
-   current thread
-   current Race Week condition
-   day/hour
-   whether the player heard the beat this Week
-   optionally, whether the account/profile has heard a rare long
    variant in recent Weeks if persistent presentation memory is desired

This does **not** need to guarantee uniqueness.

It should merely bias away from conspicuous repetition.

Example:

Three ordinary finishes in forty minutes should not all be:

> "X gets EVENT. Good race. Put that one on the board."

They might instead receive: 1. Tommy-only finish call; 2. quick
scoreboard treatment; 3. Mara performance reaction; 4. no spoken result
at all until standings recap.

**Variance includes choosing not to speak.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 145. Variance Pass II --- Density Guidance for a 72-Hour Show

Not every event needs bespoke spoken treatment.

The corpus becomes durable by varying **coverage density** as well as
wording.

Possible treatment levels:

**Level 0 --- Silent** - result exists in sim/UI/history - radio does
not mention it

**Level 1 --- Scoreboard** - one sentence result

**Level 2 --- Short reaction** - result + one observation

**Level 3 --- Conversation** - 20--60 second authored beat

**Level 4 --- Feature** - multi-minute discussion, interview, technical
analysis, retrospective

**Level 5 --- Broadcast takeover** - severe incident, title decider,
first Deep, rescue, final race, major policy event

Selection depends on salience, current thread, recent density, Week
state, player proximity/relevance, history, and whether the show has
already spent significant attention on similar material.

This is crucial for a long-session player.

If everything is Level 3, the show becomes exhausting.

If everything is Level 1, it becomes a notification feed.

The rhythm should breathe.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 146. Variance Pass II --- Long-Session Listener Protection

For the player who leaves Race Week Live on for many hours:

-   do not explain the premise repeatedly;
-   do not restate standings after every race;
-   do not repeat full biographies after a driver becomes familiar;
-   shorten identification naturally over time;
-   let jokes evolve;
-   let hosts refer back to earlier conversations;
-   use silence;
-   use music/stingers/track ambience where available;
-   permit periods where the show is mostly race calls;
-   permit periods where no race gets full coverage;
-   rotate segment voices;
-   revisit old subjects only when new evidence changes them;
-   let an unresolved question sleep for hours before returning;
-   occasionally have the hosts realize they were wrong;
-   allow minor threads to disappear without resolution;
-   do not force every authored setup to receive a payoff in the same
    session.

A player should be able to listen for six hours and feel that they spent
six hours with a broadcast team, not that they exhausted six hours of
trigger responses.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 147. Variance Pass II --- Current Completion State

This second pass substantially expands the variance corpus.

Newly covered:

-   qualifying
-   starts/restarts
-   overtakes and battles
-   records
-   odds/betting
-   provisional results and investigations
-   disqualifications
-   machine transfers
-   repairs/wear
-   schedule congestion
-   navigation/checkpoints
-   machine/class mismatch
-   public/show recognition
-   host disagreements
-   silence/restraint
-   interviews
-   broadcast mistakes/corrections
-   strange small moments
-   self-callbacks/retractions
-   day boundaries
-   NPC-only stories
-   Race Control
-   late-Week memory density
-   Moody/policy families
-   city scar tissue/cleanup
-   recurring locations
-   driver career arcs
-   series openers/finales
-   title clinches/collapses
-   aerial racing
-   first Deep
-   Deep return/reentry/rescue
-   space racing
-   healthy D4--D7
-   troubled D4--D7
-   catastrophic prolonged Week
-   final race
-   final signoff
-   Week rollover contrast
-   anti-repetition selection
-   coverage-density variance
-   long-session listener protection

The variance pass is now broad across nearly the entire current script
bank, but it is **not finished**.

The next pass should deepen the highest-repeat and highest-emotional
families rather than merely adding more categories. Priority targets:

1.  ordinary race coverage, especially live middle-of-race commentary;
2.  Day 1 ordinary-good variants, because this is the most common
    baseline;
3.  D2 memory/callback variants;
4.  D3 condition-dependent variants;
5.  severe incidents and aftermath, with careful factual boundaries;
6.  Moody events individually once the actual Moody catalog is audited;
7.  player-history beats;
8.  recurring NPC/driver/machine/location arcs;
9.  first flight → sustained flight → aerial competition progression;
10. first Deep/world-first/player-first distinction;
11. interviews once driver characterization architecture is known;
12. final-day and signoff families.

At that point the work shifts from **breadth variance** toward **depth
variance**: multiple substantially different conversations for the same
high-frequency or high-salience predicates.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 148. Delivery Context --- The Show Is an Actual Radio Station

This is a canonical presentation correction before depth variance.

Race Week Live is not an omnipresent narration layer.

It is intended to exist as an **actual NCM radio station inside
Cyberpunk's radio catalog**, available through the game's normal radio
listening context, especially the player's vehicle radio.

The player may discover it by noticing an NCM station in the station
list and choosing to listen.

There is an open implementation/design possibility that the station is
selected or playing by default in an appropriate first-entry context so
that players have a fair chance to discover it. That is **not yet a
final rule** and should not be treated as permission for the show to
follow the player everywhere.

## Consequences

-   Commentary is something the player can tune into and tune away from.
-   Race Week must remain legible through the world, UI, races,
    infrastructure, messages, and other diegetic systems even when the
    station is off.
-   The station supplements Race Week. It does not carry the entire
    burden of making Race Week exist.
-   The broadcast can be much richer because listening is voluntary.
-   Long-form conversations become more acceptable. The player
    explicitly chose the station.
-   Silence becomes even more acceptable.
-   The show should not behave as though it knows whether the player
    personally heard an earlier beat unless a separate
    presentation-memory rule intentionally tracks that.
-   "Show memory" and "listener memory" are different concepts.
-   The station may continue conceptually broadcasting while the player
    is not tuned in. Runtime implementation may realize only what the
    listener hears; canonical world simulation does not require
    synthesizing unheard audio.
-   On tuning in, the station should usually sound as though the player
    has joined an already-running broadcast rather than as though the
    hosts have been waiting for them.
-   On tuning away and returning, do not automatically recap everything
    missed.
-   Occasionally a natural recap can catch the listener up because real
    sports radio does that anyway.
-   The station should never scold the player for leaving.
-   Player-specific editorial attention remains possible because this is
    a personal dramaturgical surface, but the fiction remains a Race
    Week radio program.

## Discovery principle

**The station should feel like a delightful thing the player found
inside Race Week, not a tutorial channel stapled to the HUD.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 149. Tune-In / Tune-Out Depth Variance

Because the station lives in the radio catalog, entry into the broadcast
becomes an important high-frequency presentation state.

The system should not always fire a bespoke "welcome back" line when the
station is selected. Often the correct experience is simply joining
whatever is already happening.

## TUNE-IN-A --- Mid-race

Player selects the station during active coverage:

**TOMMY:** ...and `{DRIVER}` is still holding `{POSITION}` with
`{ATTACKER}` right there.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_CURRENT_RACE_CONTEXT}`.

No greeting. The player landed in the middle of sports radio.

## TUNE-IN-B --- Studio discussion

**JULES:** ...which is why I don't care what the rated top speed says if
the route never gives you room to use it.

**MARA:** You cared yesterday.

**JULES:** Yesterday gave me new evidence.

Again, no greeting.

## TUNE-IN-C --- News update

**IMANI:** ...effective immediately, `{SUPPORTED_POLICY_FACT}`.

**MARA:** If you're just joining us, the important part is
`{SAFE_CONCISE_RECAP}`.

Use only when the current story itself naturally merits a recap.

## TUNE-IN-D --- Quiet bed

Player may tune in to music/stinger/ambient bed before speech resumes.

Do not panic and fill the first second.

## TUNE-IN-E --- Severe ongoing story

**MARA:** If you're just joining us, we're staying with
`{SUPPORTED_EVENT}`. Confirmed right now: `{VERIFIED_FACTS}`.

Here "if you're just joining us" is justified because the story is
important enough that a broadcaster would periodically reset it.

## TUNE-IN-F --- Long race

**MARA:** Quick reset if you're coming in late: `{LEADER}` leads `{P2}`,
`{SUPPORTED_RACE_CONTEXT}`.

This should happen on broadcaster cadence, not because the game detected
a station-selection event.

## TUNE-OUT

No authored response is required.

The broadcast does not know the listener left.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 150. Depth Variance Strategy

Breadth variance asks:

> What different kinds of situations can the show cover?

Depth variance asks:

> If this situation happens fifty times, how many genuinely different
> editorial shapes can it take before the machinery becomes obvious?

High-frequency families need differences in:

-   who notices first;
-   whether the show is already committed to the race;
-   whether the event is live or recap;
-   how much of the result is spoken;
-   whether the hosts analyze, joke, argue, or simply move on;
-   whether prior history is relevant;
-   whether machine, driver, location, standings, or pure racecraft
    becomes the lens;
-   whether the show gives the moment five seconds or five minutes;
-   whether there is a callback;
-   whether there is silence;
-   whether the next event interrupts analysis after the race is
    actually complete;
-   whether no commentary occurs at all.

**Depth variance should change the shape of attention, not merely the
adjectives.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 151. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Pre-Race

Healthy ordinary racing is the baseline. It needs the largest corpus.

## ORD-PRE-A --- Bare essentials

**MARA:** `{EVENT}` coming up. `{FIELD_SIZE}` starters, `{POLE}` on
pole.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ONE-LINE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Let's go.

## ORD-PRE-B --- Route lens

**MARA:** I like this route.

**JULES:** Because?

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_ROUTE_CHARACTERISTIC}`.

**JULES:** That's going to favor `{SUPPORTED_COMPETITIVE_TRAIT}`, not
necessarily a specific driver.

**MARA:** Good. Let's find out.

## ORD-PRE-C --- Machine lens

**JULES:** Watch `{MACHINE_OR_DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** Why?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_MACHINE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** All right. You own that prediction.

## ORD-PRE-D --- Driver lens

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` has my attention.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RECENT_DRIVER_RESULT}`.

**MARA:** Exactly. I want another data point.

## ORD-PRE-E --- No obvious favorite

**MARA:** Who wins?

**JULES:** I don't know.

**MARA:** Beautiful answer.

**JULES:** You asked.

**MARA:** That's why we're watching.

## ORD-PRE-F --- Obvious favorite

**MARA:** Everybody has `{FAVORITE}`.

**JULES:** Not everybody.

**MARA:** The board has `{FAVORITE}`.

**JULES:** Better.

**MARA:** You picking against them?

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** Coward.

## ORD-PRE-G --- New names

**MARA:** I have three names on this grid I don't think we've said once.

**JULES:** Yet.

**MARA:** Exactly. Race Week keeps making introductions.

## ORD-PRE-H --- Familiar field

**MARA:** I know this group now.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_SHARED_SERIES_OR_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** Which means I have expectations. Dangerous development.

## ORD-PRE-I --- Quick turnaround

**MARA:** Didn't `{DRIVER}` just race?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_TIME_SINCE_LAST_START}`.

**MARA:** That's not enough time for me to recover and I was sitting
down.

## ORD-PRE-J --- Player absent, no issue

**MARA:** `{EVENT}` is next. `{SUPPORTED_FIELD_STORY}`.

No player reference. Ordinary world independence is itself depth.

## ORD-PRE-K --- Tommy sensory setup

**TOMMY:** Grid is forming at `{LOCATION}`.
`{SUPPORTED_VISIBLE_OR_AUDIBLE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** How's it feel down there?

**TOMMY:** `{AUTHORED_IMPRESSION_GROUNDED_IN_OBSERVABLE_CONTEXT}`.

## ORD-PRE-L --- Almost no pre-race

**MARA:** They're ready.

**JULES:** So are we.

**TOMMY:** Here we go.

Sometimes the previous segment already did enough.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 152. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Opening Phase

## ORD-OPEN-A --- Let the race breathe

**TOMMY:** They're away!

\[Track audio.\]

No studio line for several seconds.

## ORD-OPEN-B --- Immediate leader

**TOMMY:** `{LEADER}` keeps it.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_START_CONTEXT}`.

Then stop talking unless something develops.

## ORD-OPEN-C --- Position shuffle

**MARA:** That's moving.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_START_CHANGES}`.

**MARA:** Everybody settle in.

## ORD-OPEN-D --- One driver rockets forward

**TOMMY:** `{DRIVER}`!

**MARA:** Where did they come from?

**JULES:** `{START_POSITION}` to `{CURRENT_POSITION}`.

## ORD-OPEN-E --- Pole loses lead, no drama inflation

**MARA:** `{NEW_LEADER}` leads.

**JULES:** `{POLE}` back to `{POSITION}`.

**MARA:** Race changed immediately.

## ORD-OPEN-F --- Player in traffic

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is buried in it.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POSITION_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Long race.

## ORD-OPEN-G --- Calm start

Do **not** say "clean start" merely because nothing bad happened.

**TOMMY:** `{LEADER}` leads them through `{SUPPORTED_EARLY_POINT}`.

**MARA:** And we're racing.

That is enough.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 153. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Middle Phase

This is a critical high-frequency gap. The middle of a race cannot
consist only of overtakes.

## MID-A --- Gap watching

**MARA:** `{LEADER}` has `{GAP}`.

**JULES:** Growing / shrinking / stable according to supported timing.

**MARA:** That's the number I'm watching.

## MID-B --- Nothing happening is strategy

**MARA:** Everybody's holding.

**JULES:** For now.

**MARA:** You think they're waiting?

**JULES:** I know the gaps are stable. "Waiting" would be motive.

**MARA:** Right. The gaps are stable and *I* am waiting.

## MID-C --- Machine behavior

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_MACHINE_OBSERVATION}`.

**MARA:** Good or bad?

**JULES:** Observable. Give me another lap before you make me turn it
into a verdict.

## MID-D --- Route rhythm

**MARA:** I understand this race now.

**JULES:** Dangerous.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_RACE_PATTERN}`.

**JULES:** That's fair.

## MID-E --- Field compression

**MARA:** They're coming back together.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_GAP_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** Good. I was getting lonely.

## MID-F --- Field stretches

**JULES:** Field's opening up.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_GAPS}`.

**JULES:** Different races forming inside the race.

**MARA:** Pick one.

## MID-G --- Focus shifts to lower battle

**MARA:** Leader can keep doing that. Give me `{A}` and `{B}`.

**JULES:** For `{POSITION}`.

**MARA:** That's where the fight is.

## MID-H --- Standings consequence begins to matter

**JULES:** If they finish like this,
`{SUPPORTED_PROVISIONAL_POINTS_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** You did the math already?

**JULES:** Of course.

**MARA:** Nerd.

## MID-I --- No need to speak

Thirty seconds of race/engine audio can itself be the middle-race
treatment.

## MID-J --- Quickhack tension

**MARA:** `{ATTACKER}` has a target.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_HACK_STATE}`.

**MARA:** So now I watch the road and the upload.

## MID-K --- Fuel/condition question

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_FUEL_OR_CONDITION}`.

**MARA:** Is that a problem?

**JULES:** It is a fact. Whether it becomes a problem is why we're still
watching.

## MID-L --- Familiar driver style without invented psychology

**MARA:** We've seen `{DRIVER}` in this position before.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_COMPARABLE_RESULT}`.

**MARA:** Let's see if the ending rhymes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 154. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Closing Phase

## CLOSEPHASE-A --- Leader under pressure

**MARA:** Now it's close.

**JULES:** `{GAP}`.

**MARA:** That's close enough to make mistakes expensive.

## CLOSEPHASE-B --- Comfortable lead

**MARA:** `{LEADER}` just needs to bring it home.

**JULES:** "Just."

**MARA:** Fine. Complete the remaining timed distance without
surrendering `{GAP}`.

**JULES:** Much better.

## CLOSEPHASE-C --- Podium fight

**MARA:** Win might be settled. Podium isn't.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PODIUM_BATTLE}`.

**MARA:** Stay there.

## CLOSEPHASE-D --- Last points fight

**MARA:** This is for `{POSITION_OR_POINTS}`.

**JULES:** And `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_VALUE}`.

**MARA:** That's enough reason to ruin somebody's afternoon.

## CLOSEPHASE-E --- Player podium threat

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` has `{SUPPORTED_GAP}` to a podium.

**JULES:** `{REMAINING_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** That's reachable.

Only if the gap/context makes that a reasonable host opinion.

## CLOSEPHASE-F --- Player defending result

**MARA:** Don't get clever.

**JULES:** You're talking to the radio again.

**MARA:** I'm talking to myself.

This can foreshadow later direct-address ambiguity without actually
breaking format.

## CLOSEPHASE-G --- Final-lap silence

**TOMMY:** Final section.

Then let track audio carry until a decisive event.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 155. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Finish and Immediate Aftermath

## FINISH-DEEP-A --- Pure call

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` wins `{EVENT}`!

No immediate studio response.

## FINISH-DEEP-B --- Mara joy

**MARA:** That's a good race.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_RESULT_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Yeah. I liked that one.

## FINISH-DEEP-C --- Jules result lens

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_DECISIVE_FACT}`. That's where it went.

**MARA:** You already found your answer?

**JULES:** One answer.

## FINISH-DEEP-D --- Podium lens

**MARA:** `{WINNER}`, `{P2}`, `{P3}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PODIUM_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** That's your podium.

## FINISH-DEEP-E --- Someone lower down mattered more

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` wins. Hold that thought. `{DRIVER}` finished
`{POSITION}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REASON_IT_MATTERS}`.

The winner still gets acknowledged before the editorial lens shifts.

## FINISH-DEEP-F --- Machine lens

**MARA:** Win for `{WINNER}`.

**JULES:** And `{MACHINE}` just answered
`{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_MACHINE_QUESTION}`.

## FINISH-DEEP-G --- Location lens

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` gets it at `{LOCATION}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_LOCATION_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Different race here than the last one.

## FINISH-DEEP-H --- No analysis

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` wins!

\[Stinger.\]

**MARA:** Next race in `{TIME}`.

Completely legitimate.

## FINISH-DEEP-I --- Hosts disagree on quality

**MARA:** I liked that.

**JULES:** I thought `{SUPPORTED_CRITIQUE}`.

**MARA:** That's why you don't get to review restaurants.

**JULES:** Unrelated.

## FINISH-DEEP-J --- Result becomes a future hook

**MARA:** `{WINNER}` wins. Put `{DRIVER_OR_MACHINE}` in the notebook.

**JULES:** Already there.

The scheduler may later revisit only if new evidence arrives.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 156. Deep Ordinary-Race Bank --- Recaps

## RECAP-A --- Thirty seconds

**MARA:** If you missed `{EVENT}`: `{WINNER}` won,
`{SUPPORTED_DECISIVE_FACT}`, `{P2}` and `{P3}` completed the podium.

## RECAP-B --- Story recap

**MARA:** The result says `{WINNER}`. The race was really about
`{SUPPORTED_STORY}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_DETAIL}`.

## RECAP-C --- Machine recap

**NIA:** The useful thing from `{EVENT}` is
`{SUPPORTED_MACHINE_EVIDENCE}`.

**MARA:** Not the winner?

**NIA:** You already told them the winner.

## RECAP-D --- Standings recap

**JULES:** `{EVENT}` moved `{DRIVER}` from `{OLD}` to `{NEW}` in
`{SERIES}`.

**MARA:** That's the part that survives the afternoon.

## RECAP-E --- Missed-live honesty

**MARA:** We weren't on this one live. Final result:
`{SUPPORTED_RESULT}`.

Then discuss only facts NCM is permitted to know retrospectively.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 157. Day 1 Depth --- Healthy / Expected Race Week

This is the most important emotional baseline.

The show should have **lots** of ways to simply enjoy the event.

## D1-GOOD-A --- "This rules"

**MARA:** This is great.

**JULES:** Strong analysis.

**MARA:** I spent months preparing analysis. Turns out the cars started
racing and my conclusion is: this is great.

## D1-GOOD-B --- Names becoming people

**MARA:** I knew none of these names this morning.

**JULES:** You knew some.

**MARA:** Emotionally, none.

**JULES:** That's not a statistic.

**MARA:** Now `{SUPPORTED_DRIVER_HISTORY}` and I care what happens next.

## D1-GOOD-C --- Schedule abundance

**MARA:** We finish one race and there are three more things I want to
watch.

**JULES:** This was the plan.

**MARA:** I know. The plan was correct.

## D1-GOOD-D --- City as racetrack

**MARA:** I've been through `{LOCATION}` a hundred times.

**JULES:** And?

**MARA:** Never looked at it this way.

## D1-GOOD-E --- Machine diversity

**MARA:** Every grid is an argument about what a race car is.

**JULES:** The rules answer that.

**MARA:** The results answer it better.

## D1-GOOD-F --- No grand thesis

**MARA:** Next race?

**JULES:** `{EVENT}`.

**MARA:** Perfect.

A healthy Week should contain many beats this uncomplicated.

## D1-GOOD-G --- First-day overload

**MARA:** I have too many notes.

**JULES:** Throw some away.

**MARA:** Absolutely not. Somebody will become important and then I'll
look prophetic.

## D1-GOOD-H --- Host prediction game

**MARA:** Pick one.

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** Why?

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REASON}`. You?

**MARA:** `{OTHER_DRIVER}` because I like the machine.

**JULES:** That's worse methodology than I expected.

## D1-GOOD-I --- First repeat location, not ominous

**MARA:** Back to `{LOCATION}` already.

**JULES:** Different route/event if supported.

**MARA:** Good. I get a second look.

## D1-GOOD-J --- Evening

**MARA:** It feels different at night.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ENVIRONMENT_OR_CONDITION}`.

**MARA:** Same Week. Different city.

## D1-GOOD-K --- They realize they've been on air forever

**MARA:** What time is it?

**JULES:** `{TIME}`.

**MARA:** No.

**JULES:** Yes.

**MARA:** Excellent.

## D1-GOOD-L --- Simple affection

**MARA:** I wanted this to be good.

**JULES:** It is.

**MARA:** Yeah.

No foreshadowing. Let it be true.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 158. Day 1 Depth --- Slightly Below Expectations

This lane is not disaster. It is a good idea having a somewhat messy
first day.

## D1-SLIGHT-A --- Forgiving

**MARA:** Couple rough edges.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ISSUES}`.

**MARA:** First day. Fix them.

## D1-SLIGHT-B --- Organizer response

**IMANI:** Organizers have announced `{SUPPORTED_RESPONSE}`.

**MARA:** Good. That's what I want to hear. See problem, change
something.

## D1-SLIGHT-C --- Racing still wins

**MARA:** The logistics have annoyed me. The racing has not.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_GOOD_RACE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Exactly.

## D1-SLIGHT-D --- Jules practical

**JULES:** Nothing here looks unfixable.

**MARA:** Based on the facts we have.

**JULES:** Yes.

## D1-SLIGHT-E --- Mara impatient

**MARA:** They had months.

**JULES:** And then reality arrived.

**MARA:** I know. Reality should file its paperwork earlier.

## D1-SLIGHT-F --- End of day

**MARA:** Good first day with homework.

**JULES:** That's fair.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 159. Day 1 Depth --- Subpar

Now disappointment is real, but the hosts still believe Race Week can
become what they expected.

## D1-SUBPAR-A --- "This needs to get better"

**MARA:** I still like Race Week.

**JULES:** That's a defensive opening.

**MARA:** Because this needs to get better.

## D1-SUBPAR-B --- Specifics, not mood soup

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_PROBLEM_A}`, `{SUPPORTED_PROBLEM_B}`. Those are
not tiny.

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** Fix those and I can go back to yelling about lap times.

## D1-SUBPAR-C --- Good race feels welcome

**MARA:** Thank you.

**JULES:** To whom?

**MARA:** Everybody who just gave us a completely ordinary race.

**JULES:** That's where we're at?

**MARA:** For ten minutes, yes.

This requires actual accumulated concern. Never use on healthy D1.

## D1-SUBPAR-D --- Organizers still have benefit of doubt

**MARA:** I want to see what they change before tomorrow.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CURRENT_RESPONSE}`.

**MARA:** Good. Then we judge the change.

## D1-SUBPAR-E --- Darnell grounds it

**DARNELL:** You two are talking about results. Down here,
`{SUPPORTED_LOCAL_PROBLEM}`.

**MARA:** Fair.

**JULES:** What's the current status?

Then factual update.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 160. Day 1 Depth --- Bad / Catastrophic

The hosts' original belief has been contradicted by actual events.

## D1-BAD-A --- Disbelief

**MARA:** This is not what I thought today was going to be.

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** Not even close.

## D1-BAD-B --- They still cover sport

**MARA:** `{EVENT}` is starting.

**JULES:** Under `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

**MARA:** Then we're calling it. And we're keeping Imani close.

## D1-BAD-C --- Fear without invented public panic

**MARA:** I am worried.

**JULES:** About `{SUPPORTED_FACTUAL_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Yes. Me. I'm worried. I'm not speaking for the whole city.

## D1-CATA-A --- "Should this continue?"

**MARA:** I don't know if they should keep racing.

**JULES:** Official status is `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

**MARA:** I know what the official status is. I'm telling you I don't
know if I agree with it.

## D1-CATA-B --- Safety information first

**MARA:** Before the next race, Imani.

**IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_PUBLIC_SAFETY_INFORMATION}`.

**MARA:** That's the information. Follow the actual instruction.

## D1-CATA-C --- Race finish

**TOMMY:** `{WINNER}` across.

**MARA:** Everyone okay?

**TOMMY/IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_STATUS}`.

**MARA:** Good. Congratulations, `{WINNER}`.

Sport remains, but their first question has changed because history
earned it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 161. Day 2 Depth --- Memory

Day Two's defining resource is **yesterday**.

## D2-MEM-A --- Result callback

**MARA:** Yesterday `{DRIVER}` was `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_RESULT}`. Today
they're `{CURRENT_CONTEXT}`.

**JULES:** New event, but yes, that's the comparison.

## D2-MEM-B --- Prediction callback

**MARA:** You said `{SAFE_PRIOR_PREDICTION}`.

**JULES:** I remember.

**MARA:** I brought documentation.

## D2-MEM-C --- Machine callback

**NIA:** `{MACHINE}` is back after `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_MACHINE_EVENT}`.

**MARA:** That's what I wanted to see.

## D2-MEM-D --- Location callback

**MARA:** We were here yesterday.

**DARNELL/TOMMY:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_LOCATION_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Let's see what the place learned, if anything.

Only use "learned" metaphorically when infrastructure/history changed.

## D2-MEM-E --- Rival pair

**MARA:** `{A}` and `{B}` share a grid again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_HEAD_TO_HEAD}`.

**MARA:** Good. Sequel.

## D2-MEM-F --- New name amid familiar names

**MARA:** Everybody on this grid has a note next to them except
`{NEW_DRIVER}`.

**JULES:** That can change quickly.

## D2-MEM-G --- Yesterday's hero struggles

**MARA:** Race Week does not care what you did yesterday.

**JULES:** Standings care.

**MARA:** Fair. The road doesn't.

## D2-MEM-H --- Yesterday's failure rebounds

**MARA:** That's a better day for `{DRIVER}`.

**JULES:** After `{SUPPORTED_PRIOR_FAILURE}`.

**MARA:** Exactly. Weeks need second acts.

## D2-MEM-I --- Host familiarity

**MARA:** I don't need you to tell me `{DRIVER}`'s machine anymore.

**JULES:** Growth.

**MARA:** I'm becoming unbearable.

**JULES:** Becoming?

## D2-MEM-J --- Memory without overuse

Sometimes Day Two simply calls the race normally. Do not force yesterday
into every conversation merely because history exists.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 162. Day 3 Depth --- Consequence Depends on State

Day Three must not have a mandatory grim voice.

## D3-HEALTHY-A

**MARA:** Three days in and the racing keeps getting better.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_COMPETITIVE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** Everybody has data now. Everybody has history.

## D3-HEALTHY-B

**MARA:** This feels like a championship now.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_STANDINGS_CONTEXT}`.

## D3-MIXED-A

**MARA:** Good racing. Complicated Week.

**JULES:** Those can coexist.

**MARA:** They have been.

## D3-MIXED-B

**MARA:** Every result has a footnote now.

**JULES:** That's what accumulated history does.

## D3-BAD-A

**MARA:** I miss Day One.

**JULES:** Which part?

**MARA:** When my notes were mostly predictions.

## D3-BAD-B

**MARA:** We keep talking about whether races should happen instead of
who might win them.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_REASON}`.

**MARA:** I know. That's what bothers me.

## D3-CATA-A

**MARA:** If you're listening for racing, we're going to give you racing
when it happens. But public safety information comes first right now.

Then verified facts.

## D3-CATA-B

**JULES:** `{EVENT}` remains scheduled.

**MARA:** I don't have a joke for that.

No forced solemn monologue needed.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 163. Deep Player-History Variance

The player should feel increasingly legible to the show without becoming
the center of the universe.

## PLAYER-HIST-A --- First few starts

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` again.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_SMALL_SAMPLE}`.

**MARA:** Still figuring them out.

## PLAYER-HIST-B --- Machine association

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` and that `{MACHINE}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_SHARED_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** That's a pairing in my head now.

## PLAYER-HIST-C --- Changes machine

**MARA:** That's wrong.

**JULES:** What?

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` isn't in `{OLD_MACHINE}`.

**JULES:** They are in `{NEW_MACHINE}`.

**MARA:** I know. My brain objects.

## PLAYER-HIST-D --- Consistent podium threat

**MARA:** Put `{PLAYER_NAME}` near the front and I pay attention now.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PODIUM_PATTERN}`.

## PLAYER-HIST-E --- Win after drought

**MARA:** Finally.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_WINLESS_WINDOW}`.

**MARA:** That's why finally is allowed.

## PLAYER-HIST-F --- Repeated penalties

**MARA:** Another penalty.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PATTERN}`.

**MARA:** At some point this becomes part of how we talk about them.

## PLAYER-HIST-G --- Recovery reputation

**MARA:** Bad start doesn't scare me with `{PLAYER_NAME}` anymore.

**JULES:** Because `{SUPPORTED_RECOVERY_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** Evidence. Your word.

## PLAYER-HIST-H --- Collapse after strong start

**MARA:** This Week looked very different for `{PLAYER_NAME}` yesterday.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_CHANGE}`.

## PLAYER-HIST-I --- Returns to old car

**MARA:** There it is.

**JULES:** `{OLD_MACHINE}`.

**MARA:** That looks right.

## PLAYER-HIST-J --- No player mention

Even with rich player history, many player races should still be called
primarily as races.

This prevents the station from turning into an ego mirror.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 164. Deep NPC Arc Variance

With hundreds of drivers, the show should discover a small editorial
subset organically rather than pretending to track everyone equally.

## NPC-ARC-A --- Accidental recurring character

**MARA:** Why do we keep talking about `{DRIVER}`?

**JULES:** Because `{SUPPORTED_RESULTS_OR_EVENTS}` keep giving us
reasons.

**MARA:** Fair.

## NPC-ARC-B --- Quiet consistency

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}` again in `{POSITION_RANGE}`.

**MARA:** They haven't given us a huge headline.

**JULES:** They keep giving us points.

## NPC-ARC-C --- Wild variance

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` has finished `{SUPPORTED_RESULT_SEQUENCE}`.

**JULES:** Consistency is not the story.

**MARA:** Entertainment might be.

## NPC-ARC-D --- Machine becomes more famous to hosts than driver

**MARA:** The `{MACHINE}` is back.

**JULES:** Driven by `{DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** Right. Sorry. Car got top billing in my brain.

## NPC-ARC-E --- Driver changes show opinion

**MARA:** I didn't have `{DRIVER}` on my list.

**JULES:** You do now.

**MARA:** In ink.

## NPC-ARC-F --- Driver fades

A recurring driver may simply stop receiving coverage when results stop
being salient.

Do not write a "where did they go?" beat unless their absence is itself
a supported story.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 165. Deep Machine Arc Variance

## MACHINE-ARC-A --- Surprise competence

**JULES:** `{MACHINE}` keeps answering questions correctly.

**MARA:** That's a very mechanic way to say it's good.

## MACHINE-ARC-B --- One-track specialist

**MARA:** I want this car on `{ROUTE_TYPE}` every time.

**JULES:** Based on `{SUPPORTED_RESULTS}`.

## MACHINE-ARC-C --- Upgrade changes evidence

**NIA:** `{SUPPORTED_PUBLIC_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** And before the change it did `{PRIOR_RESULT}`.

**NIA:** Now we get the useful part: comparison.

## MACHINE-ARC-D --- Reliability reputation

**MARA:** I trust this thing now.

**JULES:** Machines do not require your trust.

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_FINISH_PATTERN}`.

**JULES:** Fine. Evidence supports reliability over that window.

## MACHINE-ARC-E --- Reliability collapses

**MARA:** I said I trusted it.

**JULES:** That's where you went wrong.

## MACHINE-ARC-F --- Sold/transferred

**MARA:** Same chassis, different story.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_TRANSFER_CONTEXT}`.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 166. Deep Location Arc Variance

## LOC-ARC-A --- Favorite venue

**MARA:** I like `{LOCATION}`.

**JULES:** Because the racing has been good there?

**MARA:** `{SUPPORTED_HISTORY}`. Yes.

## LOC-ARC-B --- Difficult venue

**MARA:** `{LOCATION}` keeps taking time from people.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PATTERN}`.

## LOC-ARC-C --- Infrastructure evolves

**DARNELL:** New `{BARRIER_OR_INFRASTRUCTURE}` here since `{TIME}`.

**MARA:** Does it change the race?

**JULES/TOMMY:** `{SUPPORTED_EFFECT_OR_UNKNOWN}`.

## LOC-ARC-D --- Scarred venue

**MARA:** You can see the Week here.

**DARNELL:** `{SUPPORTED_VISIBLE_CHANGES}`.

## LOC-ARC-E --- Venue recovers

**MARA:** This place looks better.

**DARNELL:** `{SUPPORTED_CLEANUP_OR_REPAIR}`.

**MARA:** Good.

## LOC-ARC-F --- New race on old scar

**MARA:** New grid, old marks.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** That's Race Week in one picture.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 167. Depth Variance --- Severe Incident Aftermath

The immediate incident and the hours afterward are different editorial
objects.

## AFTERMATH-A --- First update

**IMANI:** Update on `{INCIDENT}`: `{VERIFIED_UPDATE}`.

**MARA:** Thank you.

Then continue or pause.

## AFTERMATH-B --- Cause still unknown

**MARA:** People keep asking us what caused it.

**JULES:** We still don't know.

**MARA:** That's the answer.

## AFTERMATH-C --- Cause established

**IMANI:** `{SUPPORTED_CAUSE_OR_ATTRIBUTION}` has now been established.

**MARA:** All right. That changes what we can say.

Then discuss only the established causal scope.

## AFTERMATH-D --- Race resumes

**MARA:** They're going back racing.

**JULES:** Under `{SUPPORTED_CHANGED_CONDITIONS}`.

**MARA:** I don't know how I feel about that yet.

## AFTERMATH-E --- Mara angry later, not instantly

**MARA:** I've had time to think about `{SUPPORTED_EVENT}`.

**JULES:** And?

**MARA:** I'm angrier now.

This allows emotional processing to have time.

## AFTERMATH-F --- Jules revises engineering opinion

**JULES:** Earlier I called `{THING}` an engineering success.

**MARA:** You did.

**JULES:** I was talking too narrowly.

**MARA:** Explain.

Then a longer authored reflection grounded in consequences.

## AFTERMATH-G --- Ordinary race later

**MARA:** That was normal.

**JULES:** Yeah.

**MARA:** I needed normal.

Only after an actually abnormal event.

## AFTERMATH-H --- Stop repeating it

Once the verified facts and meaningful consequences have been covered,
the show should allow the incident to recede.

Do not mention tragedy every ten minutes merely because it remains
high-salience in the database.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 168. Depth Variance --- Recurring Quickhack Racecraft

## HACK-DEPTH-A --- Early novelty

**MARA:** I still haven't adjusted to watching somebody attack a car
with software at race speed.

**JULES:** You'll get there.

## HACK-DEPTH-B --- Tactical normalization

**JULES:** `{ATTACKER}` is holding the hack.

**MARA:** Waiting for what?

**JULES:** We know they're holding it. We don't know the intended
trigger.

**MARA:** You ruin all my verbs.

## HACK-DEPTH-C --- Known defensive driver

**MARA:** `{TARGET}` has handled this before.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_DEFENSE_HISTORY}`.

**MARA:** Doesn't make the upload free.

## HACK-DEPTH-D --- Failed attack changes battle

**MARA:** Denied.

**JULES:** And `{ATTACKER}` spent time/resources on it if supported.

**MARA:** Failed move still changes the race.

## HACK-DEPTH-E --- Total Failure before consequences known

**MARA:** That's Total Failure.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_VISIBLE_EFFECT}`.

**MARA:** Driver status?

Then facts. No theatrical assumptions.

## HACK-DEPTH-F --- Hack becomes expected rivalry tool

**MARA:** `{A}` targeting `{B}`.

**JULES:** Again, if supported.

**MARA:** At this point their races have a second lane nobody can see.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 169. Depth Variance --- Weather Across Hours

Weather should alter conversations without becoming a repetitive weather
report.

## WX-A --- First rain

**MARA:** Rain.

**JULES:** Now we learn things.

## WX-B --- Rain has persisted

**MARA:** Still wet.

**NIA:** And `{SUPPORTED_TEMPERATURE_OR_SURFACE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** So nobody gets to pretend this is the same setup problem as
two hours ago.

## WX-C --- Rain clears

**MARA:** That's stopping.

**JULES:** Road doesn't instantly forget it.

## WX-D --- Heat

**NIA:** `{SUPPORTED_ROAD_TEMP}`.

**MARA:** That's a number I can feel from the studio.

## WX-E --- Cold night

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ROAD_TEMP}`.

**MARA:** Tires are going to love us.

**NIA:** That depends on the tire.

**MARA:** I set that up for you.

## WX-F --- Weather becomes background

Once a condition persists for hours, stop announcing it every race.
Mention only when it materially affects a story or changes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 170. Depth Variance --- Standings Without Repeating the Board

## BOARD-A --- Leader only

**MARA:** `{LEADER}` leads by `{MARGIN}`.

Enough.

## BOARD-B --- Movement

**JULES:** Biggest mover from the last block: `{DRIVER}`, `{OLD}` to
`{NEW}`.

## BOARD-C --- Cluster

**MARA:** Look at `{POSITIONS}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_POINT_SPREAD}` covers all of them.

**MARA:** That's where the championship is.

## BOARD-D --- One driver's requirement

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}` needs `{SUPPORTED_REQUIREMENT}`.

**MARA:** That's easier to understand than twenty numbers.

## BOARD-E --- No standings

The scheduler may skip standings for hours if nothing meaningful
changes.

## BOARD-F --- Mara refuses full read

**MARA:** I'm not reading twenty names at you. `{LEADER}` leads,
`{SUPPORTED_BATTLE}` is tight, and the full board is there if you want
it.

This assumes the relevant UI is actually available to the
listener/player.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 171. Depth Variance --- Quiet Period Long Forms

These are reservoirs for the long-session listener.

## LONG-A --- "Why this route?"

A 2--4 minute authored discussion between Mara, Jules, and optionally
Tommy about what makes a particular supported route interesting, using
public geometry/conditions/history without inventing lore.

## LONG-B --- "What makes a good race car here?"

Mara asks Jules and Nia to disagree about the ideal Night City race
machine. The conversation may reference supported current Week examples.

## LONG-C --- "The driver nobody expected"

A retrospective on a breakout driver using only recorded results and
public biography.

## LONG-D --- "The machine we were wrong about"

Hosts revisit their own earlier expectations after enough results exist.

## LONG-E --- "What has Race Week changed?"

Darnell joins with supported infrastructure/traffic/location changes. No
invented citywide sentiment.

## LONG-F --- "Money after two days"

Celeste walks through public purses, completed transactions, sponsor
changes, and machine losses without claiming private solvency.

## LONG-G --- "How do you recover from a DNF?"

Jules discusses racing strategy generally, while Mara uses actual Week
examples. Avoid pretending to know private team plans.

## LONG-H --- "Why do people keep entering?"

A human conversation about competition using the visible fact that
entries continue, without claiming universal motives.

These should eventually receive full authored scripts, not merely topic
labels.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 172. Depth Variance --- Broadcast Rhythm Templates

The scheduler can use broad **attention rhythms**, not fixed playlists.

## RHYTHM-A --- Race-heavy 20 minutes

-   short pre-race
-   full start
-   selected middle-race moments
-   finish
-   20-second reaction
-   scoreboard from another event
-   next race

## RHYTHM-B --- Studio-heavy 20 minutes

-   result recap
-   3-minute Garage discussion
-   news update
-   quiet bed
-   qualifying result
-   host conversation
-   live join for final two minutes of race

## RHYTHM-C --- Chaotic calendar

-   one chosen live race owns thread
-   other results queue
-   finish
-   two concise catch-ups
-   urgent grid join elsewhere
-   standings deferred

## RHYTHM-D --- Quiet night

-   longer host conversation
-   sparse race calls
-   weather/technical texture
-   music/ambient gaps
-   one detailed race
-   reflective callback

## RHYTHM-E --- Bad Week

-   verified public information
-   selected race coverage
-   restrained analysis
-   longer silences
-   fewer jokes
-   periodic factual resets
-   no compulsive tragedy repetition

The point is not to choose a rigid mode every twenty minutes. These are
reference shapes for believable radio pacing.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 173. Depth Variance --- What NOT to Randomize

Some repetition is identity.

Do not create variance merely because a string repeated.

Candidates for intentional stability:

-   the fixed fresh-Week opening;
-   show/station name;
-   host names;
-   recurring segment stingers;
-   certain sign-on language;
-   Race Control terminology;
-   official classification vocabulary;
-   specific safety language where precision matters;
-   some recurring jokes;
-   certain first-time historic calls if the exact line is meant to
    become iconic.

**Variance is not synonym churn.**

A player recognizing a phrase can make the station feel more real.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 174. Depth Variance --- Coder Selection Model

The eventual implementation should conceptually select in this order:

1.  **What currently owns attention?**
2.  **Is interruption justified?**
3.  **What factual event/state is editorially worth speaking about?**
4.  **What treatment level does it deserve: 0--5?**
5.  **What temporal/Week-condition/history context applies?**
6.  **Which authored family fits?**
7.  **Which whole variant has not become conspicuously repetitive?**
8.  **Are all required facts epistemically permitted?**
9.  **Does this variant contradict something the hosts have already
    established?**
10. **Does the beat need a handoff, silence, or continuation
    afterward?**

Only then fill bounded slots.

Do **not**:

`event → random eligible line`

Do:

`attention → salience → treatment → authored scene → bounded facts`

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# 175. Depth Variance Pass Progress

This pass begins the transition from broad coverage to durable
high-frequency listening.

Deepened substantially:

-   station/tune-in presentation;
-   ordinary pre-race;
-   opening phase;
-   race middle;
-   closing phase;
-   finishes;
-   recaps;
-   healthy Day 1;
-   slightly troubled Day 1;
-   subpar Day 1;
-   bad/catastrophic Day 1;
-   Day 2 memory;
-   Day 3 state dependence;
-   player history;
-   recurring NPC arcs;
-   recurring machine arcs;
-   recurring location arcs;
-   severe-incident aftermath;
-   recurring quickhack racecraft;
-   weather over time;
-   standings treatment;
-   quiet-period long-form reservoirs;
-   broadcast rhythm;
-   intentional repetition;
-   coder selection order.

Before repo handoff, recommended remaining depth work:

1.  write several of the quiet-period long forms in full;
2.  deepen ordinary race middles further because they will consume
    enormous airtime;
3.  deepen Day 1 evening/night and Day 2 morning transitions;
4.  add more low-salience utility material that can safely recur;
5.  deepen tune-in behavior and station discovery once the exact
    Cyberpunk radio integration is known;
6.  do a final contradiction/epistemic/foreshadowing cleanup pass across
    older material;
7.  clearly label working dialogue versus hard behavioral requirements
    for the coder.

The document is approaching a useful implementation handoff state.

---

# 176. CORPUS EXPANSION PASS I — Depth Before Breadth

This pass begins the deliberate expansion from a proof-of-voice corpus into a genuinely deep broadcast reservoir.

The target is not one hundred hours of mandatory playback. The target is a library large enough that a seventy-two-hour Race Week can select a much smaller, coherent show without exposing the bottom of the bucket.

The unit remains the **whole authored beat**. These are not interchangeable sentence fragments. Each variant has its own editorial reason to exist, its own conversational shape, and its own preferred fact pattern.

For all dialogue below:

- bracketed fields are bounded canonical inputs, never invitations to invent facts;
- a variant is ineligible if its required fact is absent;
- opinions belong to the speaker;
- silence remains a valid competing treatment;
- the selector should prefer editorial diversity over lexical diversity;
- repeated facts are allowed when a real broadcaster would naturally reset context, but the *conversation* should not merely repeat itself;
- no line implies a crowd, motive, relationship, injury, cause, reputation, public reaction, or machine property unless the corresponding knowledge is available;
- ordinary races receive the greatest depth because ordinary racing is the station's daily bread.

The purpose of this pass is to make Race Week Live feel less like a trigger library and more like a place people can live in for hours.

---

# 177. Ordinary pre-race reservoir — additional whole variants

These variants assume the show has elected to give an upcoming race some attention. They do not require the race to be historically important.

## PRE-ORD-13 — The field is enough

**MARA:** All right. `{EVENT}` next.

**JULES:** `{FIELD_SIZE}` entered.

**MARA:** Anybody you love?

**JULES:** No.

**MARA:** Anybody you hate?

**JULES:** Also no.

**MARA:** Beautiful. A race with no homework. Let's see who makes us care.

## PRE-ORD-14 — One familiar name

**MARA:** I know one name in this field.

**JULES:** `{DRIVER}`.

**MARA:** That's the one.

**JULES:** Which means by the finish you'll confidently mispronounce three more.

**MARA:** That's growth.

## PRE-ORD-15 — Machine first

**JULES:** I want to watch `{MACHINE}` here.

**MARA:** Not the driver?

**JULES:** The driver too. But `{SUPPORTED_MACHINE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** There it is. He found a component to root for.

**JULES:** Components don't give interviews.

**MARA:** That's why you like them.

## PRE-ORD-16 — Route first

**MARA:** `{LOCATION}` again, and this field is going to make it look different.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ROUTE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** Which is the polite technical way of saying somebody is going to make a choice there.

**JULES:** Several people will make choices there.

**MARA:** Some of them even good.

## PRE-ORD-17 — Qualifying mattered

**MARA:** `{POLE_DRIVER}` starts first after `{SUPPORTED_QUALIFYING_FACT}`.

**JULES:** And now qualifying stops being a story and becomes a starting condition.

**MARA:** Cold.

**JULES:** Correct.

**MARA:** Fine. Let's see if they can turn it into something warmer.

## PRE-ORD-18 — Qualifying probably did not matter much

**MARA:** We have the grid.

**JULES:** We do.

**MARA:** You don't sound moved.

**JULES:** Ask me after the first timing point.

**MARA:** There is the official Julian Mercer endorsement of qualifying.

## PRE-ORD-19 — Dense schedule

**MARA:** This is one of those stretches where you look away and three races happen.

**JULES:** `{EVENT}` first.

**MARA:** So we're staying here.

**JULES:** For now.

**MARA:** Race Week is bigger than the show. Pick a window and look through it.

## PRE-ORD-20 — Quiet schedule

**MARA:** We actually have room around this one.

**JULES:** Dangerous.

**MARA:** Why?

**JULES:** You'll start having thoughts.

**MARA:** I have thoughts during the busy periods.

**JULES:** No time to prove it.

**MARA:** `{EVENT}` coming up. We can afford to watch it breathe.

## PRE-ORD-21 — No obvious favorite

**MARA:** I keep looking at this field for the obvious answer.

**JULES:** Stop looking.

**MARA:** You don't have one either?

**JULES:** Not from what we actually know.

**MARA:** Good. No fake certainty. Pick somebody after they earn it.

## PRE-ORD-22 — Favorite, but not prophecy

**MARA:** `{FAVORITE}` is the obvious name.

**JULES:** Obvious isn't inevitable.

**MARA:** I know. I'm giving the audience a handle, not engraving a trophy.

**JULES:** Then yes. `{SUPPORTED_FAVORITE_FACT}`.

**MARA:** And everybody else gets a chance to ruin the sentence.

## PRE-ORD-23 — Familiar venue, unfamiliar field

**MARA:** We know `{LOCATION}` better than we know most of these drivers.

**JULES:** That can be useful.

**MARA:** Because?

**JULES:** The place stays still long enough to show you what the field does differently.

**MARA:** There. That's what I'm watching.

## PRE-ORD-24 — Familiar field, different venue

**MARA:** I know these names. I don't know what they're going to look like here.

**JULES:** Better question anyway.

**MARA:** You really hate stable conclusions.

**JULES:** I hate conclusions that survived by avoiding new evidence.

## PRE-ORD-25 — New series introduction, restrained

**MARA:** First time we've stopped for `{SERIES}` today.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_SERIES_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That's all you get before the start. We'll learn the rest by watching it.

## PRE-ORD-26 — Late entry

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` made this field after `{SUPPORTED_ENTRY_FACT}`.

**JULES:** They're here. That's the relevant part now.

**MARA:** I like the last-minute ones.

**JULES:** Of course you do.

**MARA:** Everybody looks more interesting when the door almost closed on them.

## PRE-ORD-27 — Player enters, low ego

**MARA:** `{PLAYER_NAME}` is in this one.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_PLAYER_ENTRY_FACT}`.

**MARA:** We'll keep an eye on them. We are not turning the rest of the field invisible.

**JULES:** Thank you.

**MARA:** You say that like I personally invented protagonist syndrome.

## PRE-ORD-28 — Player absent

**MARA:** No `{PLAYER_NAME}` here.

**JULES:** Correct.

**MARA:** Good reminder that Race Week continues without asking where you are.

**JULES:** `{EVENT}` has enough problems of its own.

## PRE-ORD-29 — Short turnaround

**MARA:** `{DRIVER}` is back already.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_TURNAROUND_FACT}`.

**MARA:** That's not a story by itself.

**JULES:** No. But if the machine looks different, then we have one.

**MARA:** Eyes open, conclusions closed.

## PRE-ORD-30 — Night race setup

**MARA:** `{LOCATION}`, after dark.

**JULES:** Same route. Different information.

**MARA:** Visibility?

**JULES:** Among other things, if supported by conditions.

**MARA:** You just edited yourself on air.

**JULES:** Somebody has to.

## PRE-ORD-31 — Weather changed

**MARA:** This is not the version of `{LOCATION}` we had earlier.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_WEATHER_CHANGE}`.

**MARA:** Which means earlier notes are notes, not answers.

**JULES:** Correct.

**MARA:** I love when the city deletes our homework.

## PRE-ORD-32 — Tiny setup

**MARA:** `{EVENT}`.

**JULES:** `{SUPPORTED_ONE_LINE_CONTEXT}`.

**MARA:** That's enough. Tommy?

# 178. Start and opening-lap reservoir

The opening should not always be treated as a miniature incident report. Sometimes the correct call is simply orientation.

## OPEN-08 — Names settle into order

# 179. Ordinary race middle — major reservoir

This is one of the highest-frequency surfaces in the entire show. The middle of a race cannot exist merely as connective tissue between start and finish. It needs its own vocabulary of attention.

# 194. Quiet-period long form I — Why does a city race work at all?

Eligibility: healthy or mildly strained Week; enough established racing history to discuss several actual venues; no urgent active thread.

**MARA:** I have a question that sounds obvious until you try to answer it. Why are some of these races good?

**JULES:** That's your question?

**MARA:** Yes. Not why is racing good. Why does taking a city that was not built as a circuit and drawing a route through it sometimes produce something genuinely excellent?

**JULES:** Constraints.

**MARA:** That's one word.

**JULES:** You asked me a large question during a quiet period. I'm pacing myself.

**MARA:** Fine. Constraints.

**JULES:** A purpose-built circuit decides what problem it wants the machine to solve. A city already exists. Width, grade, surface, intersections, barriers, sight lines, all of that arrives before the race does.

**MARA:** And then Race Week says: deal with it.

**JULES:** More or less. But we should keep this grounded in the places we've actually watched. `{SUPPORTED_LOCATION_A}` gave us `{SUPPORTED_TRAIT_A}`. `{SUPPORTED_LOCATION_B}` gave us `{SUPPORTED_TRAIT_B}`.

**MARA:** And those don't reward the same thing.

**JULES:** Exactly.

**MARA:** Which is why I keep getting annoyed when somebody asks, “What's the best car?”

**JULES:** Good. You should be annoyed.

**MARA:** That sounded almost affectionate.

**JULES:** The better question is best for what, here, now, under these conditions, with this driver.

**MARA:** And with whatever happened to the car three races ago.

**JULES:** If that state persists and is known, yes.

**MARA:** There he is.

**JULES:** You invited me.

**MARA:** I did. And I think that's the part of city racing I didn't expect to love this much. The place refuses to become neutral.

**JULES:** It can't. The place is part of the problem.

**MARA:** `{LOCATION_C}` doesn't disappear because the green light comes on.

**JULES:** Right.

**MARA:** Okay. That's my answer. A good Race Week route isn't a temporary racetrack painted over Night City. It's Night City remaining stubbornly itself while everybody tries to race through it.

**JULES:** I can live with that.

**MARA:** High praise. We have `{NEXT_EVENT}` coming up in `{SUPPORTED_TIME}`.

---

# 195. Quiet-period long form II — When do you start believing a driver?

**MARA:** When do you start believing somebody is good?

**MARA:** Yes.

**JULES:** And that can become a mistake.

**JULES:** Somebody has to.

**JULES:** That's better.

**MARA:** Stop grading me.

**MARA:** Thank you. Correct record beats graceful record.

These are not triggered *because* the listener tuned in. They are authored shapes that are safe and satisfying to encounter midstream.

**JULES:** I'm saying it supports it.

**MARA:** That's not what you said thirty seconds ago.

**JULES:** Then I am improving in real time.

## TUNE-SAFE-02 — Mid-scoreboard

**MARA:** ...`{RESULT_B}`. And `{RESULT_C}` just settled as `{RESULT_C_VALUE}`. The one we're watching now is `{ACTIVE_EVENT}`.

## TUNE-SAFE-03 — Mid-race

**TOMMY:** ...gap is `{GAP}` now, and `{CHALLENGER}` has been taking pieces out of it over `{SUPPORTED_WINDOW}`.

**MARA:** That's the first time I'm willing to call the trend.

## TUNE-SAFE-04 — Mid-technical discussion

**NIA:** ...which is why I don't care what the part is called. If the observed effect is `{SUPPORTED_EFFECT}`, that's where I start.

**JULES:** Exactly.

**MARA:** I regret bringing both of you together.

## TUNE-SAFE-05 — Mid-joke, then context

**MARA:** ...and that's why you're banned from naming anything.

## TUNE-SAFE-06 — Quiet bed into context

[Music/bed for several seconds.]

**MARA:** Current leader in `{SERIES}` is `{LEADER}`. Next race we're carrying is `{EVENT}`. That's where we are.

This is a natural broadcaster reset, not listener detection.
